## Maritime Occupational Health and Safety Regulations {#maritime-occupational-health-and-safety-regulations .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/2010-120
:::

[CANADA LABOUR CODE](/eng/acts/L-2)

Registration 2010-06-03

Maritime Occupational Health and Safety Regulations

P.C. 2010-707 2010-06-03
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister of Labour and the Minister of Transport, pursuant to
section 157[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_610727-E_hq_7321){#fn_610727-E_hq_7321-ID0EGCAA}
of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#fn_610727-E_hq_7322){#fn_610727-E_hq_7322-ID0EDCAA},
hereby makes the annexed [Maritime Occupational Health and Safety
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-120).

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_610727-E_hq_7321-ID0EGCAA){#fn_610727-E_hq_7321}S.C.
  2000, c. 20, s. 20
  :::

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote

  L-2
  :::
:::

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-755550 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755551 .Subheading}

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in these Regulations.

[[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Part II of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ANSI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ANSI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the American National Standards
    Institute. ([ANSI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[approved organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[approved organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an organization
    that is approved by any province for the teaching of first aid, or a
    recognized institution within the meaning of subsection 1(1) of the
    [Marine Personnel
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115). ([organisme
    agréé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[confined space]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[confined space]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an enclosed or
    partially enclosed space that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not designed or intended for human occupancy,
      except for the purpose of performing work;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has restricted means of access and egress; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may become hazardous to any person entering it
      owing to

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} its design, construction, location or
        atmosphere,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the materials or substances in it, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any other conditions relating to it. ([espace
        clos]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[crew accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[crew accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means living, eating,
    recreational or sleeping quarters provided by an employer for the
    accommodation of employees on a vessel. ([logement de
    l'équipage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[CSA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[CSA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Standards
    Association. ([CSA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[day vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[day vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is not
    equipped with accommodation to provide rest for
    employees. ([bâtiment de jour]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[disabling injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[disabling injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employment injury
    or an occupational illness that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents an employee from reporting for work or
      from effectively performing all the duties connected with their
      regular work on any day after the day on which the injury or
      illness occurred, whether or not that later day was a work day for
      that employee;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} results in an employee's loss of a body member or
      part of one or in the complete loss of the usefulness of a body
      member or part of one; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} results in the permanent impairment of a body
      function of an employee. ([blessure invalidante]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[electrical equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[electrical equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means equipment for the
    generation, distribution or use of electricity. ([outillage
    électrique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the certificate
    issued by or with the authority of an approved organization for
    successful completion of a two-day first aid course. ([certificat de
    secourisme]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[gross tonnage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[gross tonnage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the volume of a vessel
    as determined by a tonnage measurer or calculated in accordance with
    the regulations made under paragraph 77(h) of the Canada Shipping
    Act, 2001. ([jauge brute]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ILO]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ILO]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the International Labour
    Organization. ([OIT]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[IMO]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[IMO]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the International Maritime
    Organization. ([OMI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[inland voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inland voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage en eaux
    internes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[inland waters of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inland waters of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
    as in section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([eaux internes du
    Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[international voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[international voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a voyage between
    a place in Canada and a place not in Canada or between places not in
    Canada. ([voyage international]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[isolation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[isolation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means separation or disconnection
    from every source of energy that is capable of making equipment
    dangerous. ([isolation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[lock out]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[lock out]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means placement of a lock on
    equipment, machines or energy-isolating devices in accordance with
    an established procedure, to indicate that the equipment, machines
    or devices are not to be operated until the lock is removed in
    accordance with an established
    procedure. ([cadenasser]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[marine chemist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[marine chemist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person who

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a graduate of a post-secondary education
      institution who

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has successfully completed courses in chemical
        engineering,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has successfully completed a general program
        with a major in chemistry, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is a member of the Chemical Institute of
        Canada; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has at least three years' experience in chemical
      or engineering work, after the person has satisfied the
      requirements of paragraph (a), of which a minimum of 150 working
      hours were spent under the supervision of a qualified person in
      work on board a vessel testing tank reservoirs and other
      containers in the application of gas hazard control
      standards. ([chimiste de la marine]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[MLC 2006]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[MLC 2006]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Maritime Labour
    Convention, 2006. ([CTM 2006]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[near coastal voyage, Class 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[near coastal voyage, Class 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
    meaning as in section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage à proximité du
    littoral, classe 1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[NIOSH]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[NIOSH]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National Institute for
    Occupational Safety and Health in the United
    States. ([NIOSH]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[oxygen deficient atmosphere]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[oxygen deficient atmosphere]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    atmosphere in which there is less than 19.5 per cent by volume of
    oxygen at a pressure of one atmosphere or in which the partial
    pressure of oxygen is less than 148 mm Hg. ([air à faible teneur en
    oxygène]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2023-257, s. 400\]]{.Repealed}

[[personal service room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[personal service room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a change room,
    sanitary facility, shower room, or other area of the crew
    accommodation or a combination of those areas. ([local réservé aux
    soins personnels]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[protection equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[protection equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means safety materials,
    equipment, devices and clothing. ([équipement de
    protection]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[qualified person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[qualified person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
    specified duty, a person who, because of their knowledge, training
    and experience, is qualified to perform that duty safely and
    properly. ([personne qualifiée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
    document, present and easily accessible at the work place at all
    times. ([facilement accessible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[rest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[rest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a period during which an employee
    is not assigned any duty and has access to sleeping quarters and
    sanitary facilities. ([repos]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sanitary facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sanitary facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a room that contains
    a water closet or a urinal. ([cabinet de toilette]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[SOLAS]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[SOLAS]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the 2004 consolidated edition of
    the International Convention for Safety of Life at Sea,
    1974. ([SOLAS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[special purpose vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[special purpose vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2023-257, s. 400\]]{.Repealed}

[[unlimited voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[unlimited voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage
    illimité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ventilation equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ventilation equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fan, blower,
    induced draft or other ventilation device used to force a supply of
    fresh, respirable atmospheric air into an enclosed space or to move
    ambient air from that space. ([matérial de
    ventilation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section 2
    of the [Canada Shipping Act,
    2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15). ([bâtiment]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
interpreting any standard incorporated by reference into these
Regulations, the use of "should" is to be read as denoting an
obligation.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755606 .Subheading}

**[[2.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2.1 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations apply
to any person who is not an employee but who performs for an employer to
which these Regulations apply activities whose primary purpose is to
enable the person to acquire knowledge or experience, and to the
employer, as if that person were an employee of the employer, and every
provision of these Regulations must be read accordingly.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-211, s. 4
:::

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations apply to
employees employed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on vessels registered in Canada;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on uncommissioned vessels of Her Majesty in right of
  Canada; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the loading or unloading of vessels.

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755614 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 217

\]
:::

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  employer is required by section 125 or 125.1 of the Act to the
  employer must keep and maintain the record and make it readily
  available for examination by the Head of Compliance and Enforcement,
  the policy committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work
  place committee or the health and safety representative for the vessel
  to which it applies.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} To comply with subsection (1), the employer may use
  any recording system, including electronic records, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} measures are taken to ensure that the records
    contained in the recording systems are protected, by electronic or
    other means, against inadvertent loss or destruction and against
    tampering; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the records contained in the recording
    systems can be printed on paper and provided on reasonable notice at
    the request of the Head of Compliance and Enforcement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-148, s. 29
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 218](#1177930-1189643){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177930-1189643.html"}
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 11](#1295174-1312265){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295174-1312265.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inconsistent Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755622 .Subheading}

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** In the event of an
inconsistency between any standard incorporated by reference in these
Regulations and any other provision of these Regulations, that other
provision must prevail to the extent of the inconsistency.

### [Incorporation by Reference]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755624 .Subheading}

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** Any reference to a standard
incorporated by reference in these Regulations is a reference to the
standard, as amended from time to time.

### [Health and Safety]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755626 .Subheading}

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} arrange that work in a work area is carried out in
    a manner that does not endanger the health or safety of any person
    who is engaged or working in that area or in connection with the
    work; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} adopt and carry out appropriate procedures and
    techniques designed or intended to prevent or reduce the risk of
    employment injury in the operation or carrying out of the work.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Crew accommodation must meet the requirements of the
  related provisions on health and safety protection and accident
  prevention, with respect to preventing the risk of exposure to
  hazardous levels of noise and vibration and other ambient factors and
  chemicals on board vessels, and provide an acceptable occupational and
  on-board living environment for employees.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer or owner must ensure that a qualified
  person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is in charge in every work area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inspects every work area or structure and every
    item of machinery or equipment to ascertain that safe working
    conditions are maintained.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person must not use any structure, machinery or
  equipment that has been reassembled after being dismantled, in whole
  or in part, until it has been examined by a qualified person and found
  to be in a safe condition.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 219](#1177932-1189646){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177932-1189646.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 16](#1299303-1300382){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299303-1300382.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 7"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Structures]{.HTitleText1} {#h-755636 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755637 .Subheading}

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Part.

[[accommodation ladder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[accommodation ladder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a means of access
    to and egress from a vessel that may include platforms on different
    levels with ladders between the platforms and that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is suspended by a supporting structure of chains
      or steel wire ropes from its lowest suspension point;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is hinged at its top; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} can be moved so that the lowest platform is
      accessible from shore. ([échelle de coupée]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[scaffold]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[scaffold]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a working platform supported
    from below. ([échafaudage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[stage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[stage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a working platform supported
    from above. ([plate-forme suspendue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755645 .Subheading}

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to
permanent and temporary structures, including means of access, gangways,
scaffolds, stages, ladders, guardrails, toe boards and safety nets.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 220](#1177937-1189653){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177937-1189653.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 9"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Design and Construction]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755647 .Subheading}

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** The employer must ensure
that the design and construction of every structure on a vessel meets
the applicable requirements set out in one or more of the following
regulations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} [Vessel Construction and Equipment
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [Hull Construction
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1431); or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [Small Vessel
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1487).

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 401\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 401](#1431174-1435326){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431174-1435326.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Temporary Structure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755653 .Subheading}

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee must not use a temporary structure on a vessel if it is
  feasible to use a permanent structure.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee must not use a temporary structure unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have been trained and instructed in its safe
    and proper use; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have their employer's authority to use it.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Tools, equipment and materials used on a temporary
  structure must be arranged or secured in a manner that they cannot be
  knocked off the structure accidentally.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A qualified person must make a visual inspection of
  the temporary structure before each work period in which a temporary
  structure is used by an employee.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} When a temporary structure is used on a vessel and
  that structure may be affected by the ingress and egress of the tide,
  a qualified person must make a visual inspection of the structure at
  the end of each tide cycle.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the inspection reveals a defect or condition that
  adversely affects the structural integrity or safe use of a temporary
  structure, a person must not use the structure until the defect or
  condition is remedied.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 221](#1177939-1189655){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177939-1189655.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Means of Access]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755663 .Subheading}

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Means
  of access used to board and disembark from a vessel must provide a
  safe passage between the vessel and shore or between two vessels, as
  the case may be.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel that is moored alongside a wharf or
  another vessel must be fitted with at least one means of access
  between the vessel and the wharf or the other vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a means of access leads to a location on board a
  vessel that is more than 0.35 m above the deck, safe access to the
  deck must be provided by means of a series of steps, a ladder or other
  similar structure.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The series of steps, ladder or similar structure must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be firmly secured to the bulwark so as to prevent
    its shifting, slipping or pivoting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be aligned with the means of access to the vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have treads that are at least 600 mm in width and
    200 mm in depth, with a non-slip surface; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with two handhold stanchions that are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} not less than 40 mm in diameter,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} extended not less than 1.2 m above the top of
      the bulwark, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} fitted at the point of boarding or disembarking
      the vessel not less than 700 mm and not more than 800 mm apart.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every accommodation ladder and gangway must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be maintained in a safe condition;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be installed in a manner that reduces movement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be rigged and maintained in position to compensate
    for the movement of the vessel;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be lighted in accordance with the [Vessel
    Construction and Equipment
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257);

  - [e)]{.lawlabel} if feasible, be adjusted in such a way that,
    whatever the state of the tide or the draught of the ship, the
    accommodation ladder's angle or the gangway's angle to the
    horizontal plane is not more than 40°;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} be provided with a lifebuoy that has an attached
    line and is strategically placed and ready for immediate use; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} meet the requirements of one of the following
    standards:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ISO Standard 5488:1979, Shipbuilding --
      Accommodation ladders,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN/CSA-S826 SERIES-01 (R2006),
      Ferry Boarding Facilities,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the applicable standard of a classification
      society that has been furnished with a certificate by the Minister
      of Transport under subsection 12(2) of the [Canada Shipping Act,
      2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15), or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} if the ladder is part of pilot transfer
      equipment referred to in Regulation 23 of Chapter V of SOLAS, IMO
      Resolution A.889(21), Pilot Transfer Arrangements.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 5(g) does not apply to accommodation
  ladders or gangways constructed prior to the coming into force of
  these Regulations.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every means of access must be thoroughly examined by
  a qualified person at least once every year by a detailed visual
  examination supplemented, if necessary, by non-destructive testing,
  dismantling of components, measurement of corrosion, deformation and
  wear, evaluation of structural and moving parts under working
  conditions and any other means, in order to arrive at a reliable
  conclusion as to the safety of the means of access and to ensure that
  the following conditions are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the loose gear used with it is in good working
    order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the parts that align and swivel under load are
    free;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the mechanical, electrical, gearing, hydraulic and
    pneumatic systems are in good working order;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} parts are not affected by corrosion to the extent
    that they are no longer functional; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} no defects or signs of permanent deformation are
    detected.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a means of access is being used by persons to
  board or disembark from a vessel, at least one end of it must be
  fastened securely and, if necessary to minimize its movement, an
  employee, other than an employee engaged in manoeuvring the vessel,
  must be stationed to assist the persons using it.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} A safety net must be fitted under every part of a
  ladder, accommodation ladder or gangway, except if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ladder or gangway and the approaches to it are
    constructed in a manner that makes the fitting of a safety net
    unnecessary; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fitting of a safety net is not possible.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} Every safety net referred to in subsection (9) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} extend on both sides of the ladder, accommodation
    ladder or gangway for a distance of 1.8 m;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be kept taut at all times;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if feasible, protect the entire length of the means
    of access; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} meet the standards referred to in section 17.

- [(11)]{.lawlabel} If a platform is provided at the bottom of a means
  of access, the platform must be flat and horizontal.

::: HistoricalNote

### [Scaffolds]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755705 .Subheading}

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise permitted by this Part, a scaffold must meet the
  requirements set out in one or more of the following standards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ANSI/ASSE Standard A10.8-2001, Scaffolding Safety
    Requirements;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ANSI/ALI Standard A14.7-2006, American National
    Standard for Mobile Ladder Stands and Mobile Ladder Stand Platforms;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z271-98 (R2004), Safety Code
    for Suspended Elevating Platforms.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The erection, use, dismantling and removal of a
  scaffold must be carried out by or under the supervision of a
  qualified person.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a scaffold is erected on an uneven surface, it
  must be provided with base plates that maintain its stability.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every scaffold must be capable of supporting at least
  four times the load that is likely to be imposed on it.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every scaffold must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have a platform that is at least 500 mm wide and
    securely fastened in place; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a flat and horizontal working surface.

### [Stages]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755717 .Subheading}

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  stage must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have a flat and horizontal working surface capable
    of supporting any load that is likely to be imposed on it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be fitted with a means of holding the stage away
    from the working area.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The supporting structure and the ropes or tackle
  supporting a stage must have a safety factor of not less than six.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 18](#1299310-1300389){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299310-1300389.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 14"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Ladders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755723 .Subheading}

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Commercially manufactured portable
  ladders must meet the requirements set out in one or more of the
  following standards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN3-Z11-M81 (R2005), Portable
    Ladders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ANSI/ALI Standard A14.1 -2007, American National
    Standard for Ladders - Wood - Safety Requirements; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ANSI/ALI Standard A14.2 -2007, American National
    Standard for Ladders - Portable Metal - Safety Requirements.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every portable ladder, while being used, must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} placed on a firm footing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} secured in a manner that it cannot be dislodged
    accidentally from its position.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every ladder, whether portable or permanently
  secured, must be positioned in a manner so that it is not necessary
  for a person to use the underside of the ladder.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a ladder provides access from one level to
  another, the ladder must extend, if feasible, at least three rungs
  above the higher level or, if it is not feasible, handholds must be
  provided.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A metal or wire rope ladder must not be used if there
  is a hazard that it may come into contact with any live electrical
  circuit or electrical equipment.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} An employee must not work from any of the three top
  rungs of any single or extension portable ladder or from either of the
  two top steps of any step ladder.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A non-metallic portable ladder must not be painted.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} Every rope ladder must be of sufficient length to
  reach the intended landing point, unless the distance from the water
  to the point of access is more than 9 m, in which case a rope ladder
  is not to be used.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} The means of attaching a rope ladder to a vessel must
  be securely rigged and maintained in a safe condition.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} A rope ladder must be equipped with flat wooden
  treads --- at regular intervals with treads wider than the width of
  the ladder --- and be installed in a manner that reduces movement of
  the ladder.

- [(11)]{.lawlabel} A rope ladder must not be used to provide access to
  places on shore except in case of emergency.

- [(12)]{.lawlabel} When it is necessary to ensure safety, a person must
  be stationed at the bottom of a ladder to assist the person using the
  ladder.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 223](#1177959-1189666){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177959-1189666.html"}

### [Guardrails and Toe Boards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755742 .Subheading}

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  raised structure or a deck opening that has a coaming height of less
  than 900 mm, from which there is a drop of more than 1.2 m, and to
  which an employee has access, must have a guardrail.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every guardrail must consist of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a horizontal top rail or line not less than 900 mm
    and not more than 1100 mm above the base of the guardrail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a horizontal intermediate rail or line spaced
    midway between the top rail or line and the base of the guardrail;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} supporting posts spaced not more than 3 m apart at
    their centres.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every guardrail must be designed to withstand the
  greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maximum load that is likely to be imposed on
    it, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a static load of not less than 890 N applied in any
    direction at any point on the top rail or line.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If there is risk that tools or other objects could
  fall from a scaffold, a stage or any other raised structure onto a
  person, the employer must install

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a toe board that extends from the floor of the
    platform or other raised structure to a height of not less than 125
    mm; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a solid or mesh panel that extends from the floor
    of the platform or other raised structure to a height of not less
    than 450 mm, if the tools or other objects are piled to a height
    that a toe board will not prevent them from falling.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the installation of a toe board is not feasible on
  a scaffold, a stage or any other raised structure, all tools or other
  objects that could fall on a person must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tied in a manner that will protect any persons
    beneath, if the tools or other objects fall; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} placed in a way that they will be caught by a
    safety net positioned so as to protect from injury any persons on or
    below the raised area, if the tools or the other objects fall.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 224](#1177964-1189669){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177964-1189669.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Safety Nets]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755758 .Subheading}

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** The design, construction
and installation of a safety net referred to in subsection 12(9),
paragraphs 16(5)(b) and 147(1)(b) must meet the standards set out in
ANSI/ASSE Standard A10.11-1989 (R1998), Safety Requirements for Safety
Nets.

### [Housekeeping and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755760 .Subheading}

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  feasible, the working surface used by an employee must be kept free of
  grease, oil or any other slippery substance and of any material or
  object that may create a hazard to an employee.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every work area used by an employee must be kept free
  of accumulations of ice and snow while the area is in use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 225](#1177969-1189673){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177969-1189673.html"}
:::

:::

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Crew Accommodation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-755764 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755765 .Subheading}

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies to crew accommodation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following provisions do not apply in respect of a
  day vessel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 20 to 40;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 42 to 45; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sections 51 to 54.

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s.
  409\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must provide a set of clean bedding to
  be used on board a vessel during the employee's service.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The bedding set must, at a minimum, consist of the
  following items of appropriate size for the berth:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one pillow;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one pillow case;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} two flat bedsheets; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} one blanket.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employee must return the bedding set on
  completion of service on board a vessel.

### [Galleys and Dining Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755886 .Subheading}

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  employee is required to eat on board a vessel there must be, if
  feasible, a galley or dining area that is equipped with, at a minimum,
  the following items:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 411\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a microwave oven;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a toaster;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a refrigerator or a cooler;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} dish washing facilities; and

  - [f)]{.lawlabel} pots, pans, strainers, dishes and utensils, in
    sufficient number to accommodate the greatest number of employees
    that could use them at any one time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every dining area provided by the employer must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 411\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided with non-combustible covered receptacles
    for the disposal of food waste or garbage; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} separated from any place where a hazardous
    substance may contaminate food, dishes or utensils.

::: HistoricalNote

### [Recreational Facilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755941 .Subheading}

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** If an employee is
required to live on board a vessel, a recreational facility must be
furnished at a minimum with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a bookcase that contains vocational and other books,
  the inventory of which must be adequate for the duration of the voyage
  and changed at reasonable intervals together with a facility for
  reading and writing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a radio capable of receiving broadcast on bands such
  as AM/FM/SW; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a television set equipped with electronic equipment
  capable of showing films, the inventory of which must be adequate for
  the duration of the voyage and changed at reasonable intervals.

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 415\]]{.Repealed}

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Sanitation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-755984 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755985 .Subheading}

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[communicable disease]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
section 2 of the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-755987 .Subheading}

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employer must maintain each personal service room, galley and pantry
  used by employees in a clean and sanitary condition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each personal service room and galley must be cleaned
  at least once every day that it is used.

<!-- -->

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  vessel is in operation, an inspection must be made once a week of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the supplies of food and water on the vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all spaces and equipment used for the storage and
    handling of food; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the galley and equipment used for the preparation
    and service of food.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record of each inspection, made in accordance with
  subsection (1), must be kept by the employer on the vessel for a
  period of three years after the day on which the inspection is made.

**[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** All cleaning and sweeping
that may cause dusty or unsanitary conditions must be carried out in a
manner that will prevent the contamination of the air by dust or other
substances injurious to health.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 251(F)](#1178055-1189755){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178055-1189755.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-63-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** If an interior deck on a
vessel is normally wet and employees on the vessel do not use non-slip
waterproof footwear, the deck must be covered with a dry false floor or
platform or treated with a non-slip product or substance.

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** Each container that is
used for solid or liquid waste in a work place must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with a tight-fitting cover;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} be constructed so that it can be easily cleaned and
  maintained in a sanitary condition;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} be leak-proof; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if there may be internal pressure in the container,
  be designed so that the pressure is relieved by controlled
  ventilation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 252(F)](#1178057-1189757){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178057-1189757.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  feasible, each enclosed part of a work place, personal service room,
  galley or pantry must be constructed, equipped and maintained in a
  manner that will prevent the entry of vermin.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If vermin have entered any enclosed part of a work
  place, personal service room, galley or pantry, the employer must
  immediately take all steps necessary to eliminate the vermin and
  prevent their re-entry.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 253](#1178059-1189759){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178059-1189759.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** A person must not use a
personal service room for the purpose of storing equipment or supplies
unless a closet fitted with a door is provided in that room for that
purpose.

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  reasonable steps must be taken to ensure that personal service rooms
  are kept mold and mycosis free.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 416\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 254(F)](#1178064-1189762){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178064-1189762.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 30(F)](#1299342-1300419){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299342-1300419.html"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 416](#1431199-1435587){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431199-1435587.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-68-20210604.html "Link to previous version of section 68"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 417\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-69-20210604.html "Link to previous version of section 69"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Sanitary Facilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756012 .Subheading}

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  every sanitary facility, the employer must provide

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} toilet paper on a holder or in a dispenser in each
    toilet compartment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} powdered or liquid soap or other sanitizing agent
    in a dispenser at each wash basin or between adjoining wash basins;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sufficient sanitary hand drying facilities to serve
    the number of employees using the sanitary facility;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a non-combustible container for used disposable
    towels if disposable towels are provided;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} menstrual products, including clean and hygienic
    tampons and menstrual pads; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a covered container lined with a bag that is
    impervious to moisture for the disposal of menstrual products.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to provide menstrual products
  in a sanitary facility, the employer must provide them in another
  location in the same work place that is controlled by the employer,
  accessible by employees at all times and offers a reasonable amount of
  privacy.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-78, s. 4](#1397693-1430624){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1397693-1430624.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Wash Basins]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756019 .Subheading}

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** In every personal service
room that contains a wash basin, the employer must provide

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} powdered or liquid soap or other sanitizing agent in
  a dispenser at each wash basin or between adjoining wash basins;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} sufficient sanitary hand drying facilities to serve
  the number of employees using the personal service room; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a non-combustible container for the disposal of used
  towels if towels are provided.

### [Showers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756024 .Subheading}

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  shower must be provided with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hot and cold water; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} soap or other sanitizing agent.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If duck-boards are used in showers, they must not be
  made of wood.

### [Water]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756030 .Subheading}

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employer must ensure that employees are provided with potable water
  for drinking, personal washing and food preparation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The potable water must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in sufficient quantity to meet the purposes set
    out in subsection (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meet the quality guidelines set out in the most
    recent edition of Guidelines for Canadian Drinking Water Quality,
    prepared by the Federal-Provincial-Territorial Committee on Drinking
    Water and published by the Department of Health.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Potable water for drinking must be available at all
  times for the use of every employee working on the vessel.

<!-- -->

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employer must develop a potable water quality management program that
  sets out the testing procedures and frequency and the measures to be
  taken to prevent contamination.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The potable water management program must be made
  readily available for inspection.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 255](#1178066-1189764){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178066-1189764.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 74"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  vessel of 300 gross tonnage or more that is not a day vessel must have
  on board a supply of water that is available for all wash basins, tubs
  and showers and is sufficient to provide at least 68 l of water for
  each employee on the vessel for each day that the employee spends on
  that vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A day vessel must have on board at least 22.7 l of
  water for each employee on the vessel for each day that the employee
  spends on that vessel.

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** If it is necessary to
transport water for drinking, personal washing or food preparation, only
sanitary portable water containers must be used.

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** If a portable storage
container for drinking water is used,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the container must be securely closed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the container must be used only for storing potable
  water;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the container must not be stored in a sanitary
  facility; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the water must be drawn from the container by

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a tap,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ladle used only for the purpose of drawing water
    from the container, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any other means that precludes the contamination
    of the water.

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** Any ice that is added to
drinking water or used for the contact refrigeration of foodstuffs must
be made from potable water, and stored and handled so as to prevent
contamination.

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** If drinking water is
supplied by a drinking fountain,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the fountain must meet the standards set out in the
  Air-Conditioning and Refrigeration Institute (ARI) standard ARI
  1010-2002, entitled Self-Contained, Mechanically-Refrigerated
  Drinking-Water Coolers, as amended from time to time; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fountain must not be installed in a sanitary
  facility.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 18](#1455646-1498710){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455646-1498710.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Preparation, Handling, Storage and Serving of Food]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756056 .Subheading}

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** Each food handler must be
trained and instructed in food handling practices that prevent the
contamination of food.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 256(F)](#1178068-1189767){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178068-1189767.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-80-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 80"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** A person who is suffering
from a communicable disease must not must work as a food handler.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 256(F)](#1178068-1189769){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178068-1189769.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2024-118, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-82-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 82"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Foods
  that require refrigeration to prevent them from becoming hazardous to
  health must be maintained at a temperature of 4°C or lower.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Foods that require freezing to prevent them from
  becoming hazardous to health must be maintained at a temperature of
  --18°C or lower.

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** All equipment and
utensils that come into contact with food must be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} designed to be easily cleaned;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} smooth, free from cracks, crevices, pitting or
  unnecessary indentations; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} cleaned and stored to maintain their surfaces in a
  sanitary condition.

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** No person must eat,
prepare or store food in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a place where a hazardous substance may contaminate
  food, dishes or utensils;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a personal service room that contains a water closet,
  urinal, tub or shower; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other place where food may be contaminated.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 257](#1178070-1189771){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178070-1189771.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 85"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Garbage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756071 .Subheading}

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** Food waste or garbage
must not be stored in a galley.

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** Garbage must be held in
leak-proof, non-absorptive, easily cleaned containers with tight-fitting
covers.

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** Dry food waste and
garbage must be removed or incinerated.

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Food
  waste and garbage containers must be kept covered and the food waste
  and garbage removed as frequently as is necessary to prevent
  unsanitary conditions.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Food waste and garbage containers must be cleaned and
  disinfected in an area separate from the galley each time they are
  emptied.

## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Safe Occupancy of the Work Place]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756078 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756079 .Subheading}

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2020-130, s. 49\]]{.Repealed}

Version](section-90-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 90"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fire Protection Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756082 .Subheading}

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** Fire protection equipment
must be installed, inspected and maintained on board every vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-14, s. 414
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 32](#1299346-1300423){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299346-1300423.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-91-20170203.html "Link to previous version of section 91"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Emergency Evacuation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756088 .Subheading}

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** Emergency evacuation
equipment must be installed, inspected and maintained on board every
vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 32](#1299346-1300426){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299346-1300426.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-92-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 92"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Emergency Procedures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756090 .Subheading}

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employer must prepare emergency procedures, including evacuation
  procedures.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notices that set out the details of the emergency
  procedures must be posted in conspicuous places that are accessible to
  every employee in the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 33](#1299348-1300429){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299348-1300429.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-93-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Training and Instruction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756094 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 259(F)

\]
:::

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** Every employee must be
trained and instructed in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed by an employee in the
  event of an emergency; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the location, use and operation of fire protection
  equipment and emergency equipment provided by the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 260(F)](#1178079-1189780){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178079-1189780.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-94-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Inspections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756098 .Subheading}

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  visual inspection of every vessel must be carried out by a qualified
  person at least once every six months and must include an inspection
  of all fire escapes, exits and stairways and fire protection equipment
  on board the vessel in order to ensure that they are in serviceable
  condition and ready for use at all times.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record of each inspection must be dated and signed
  by the person who carried out the inspection and kept by the employer
  on board the vessel for a period of two years after the day on which
  it is signed.

## [PART 6]{.HLabel1}[Medical Care]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756190 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756191 .Subheading}

**[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[detached work place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[detached work place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a work place away
    from a vessel where employees normally employed on the vessel are
    engaged in work related to the operation of the vessel for extended
    periods of time. ([lieu de travail isolé]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[first aid room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[first aid room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a room used exclusively
    for first aid or medical purposes. ([salle de premiers
    soins]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[health unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[health unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a consultation and
    treatment facility that is in the charge of a person who is a
    registered nurse under the laws of any province. ([service de
    santé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[medical facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[medical facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a medical clinic or
    the office of a physician. ([installation médicale]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[medicine chest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[medicine chest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a container in which an
    assortment of medicines is stored. ([pharmacie de
    bord]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756198 .Subheading}

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2021-122, s. 34\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-106-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 106"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel must carry a complete and up-to-date list of radio stations
  from which medical advice can be obtained.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel is equipped with a system of satellite
  communication, it must carry a complete and up-to-date list of coast
  earth stations from which medical advice can be obtained.

**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** Employees with
responsibility for medical care or first aid must be instructed by the
employer in the use of the ship's medical guide and in the medical
section of the most recent edition of the International Code of Signals
so that they can understand the type of information needed by the
advising doctor and the advice received.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 269(F)](#1178126-1189809){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178126-1189809.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-108-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 108"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** Every employer must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish written instructions that provide for the
  prompt rendering of first aid to an employee for any injury, disabling
  injury or illness;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} make a copy of the instructions readily available for
  examination by employees; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a cargo that is classified dangerous, within the
  meaning of the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act,
  1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01), has not been included in the most recent
  edition of the Medical First Aid Guide for Use in Accidents Involving
  Dangerous Goods published by the IMO, ILO and World Health
  Organization, make readily available to employees the necessary
  information on the nature of the substances in the cargo, the risks
  involved, the necessary personal protection equipment required, the
  relevant medical procedures and specific antidotes.

::: HistoricalNote

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** If an employee sustains
an injury or becomes aware that they have a disabling injury or illness,
the employee must, if feasible, report immediately for first aid to a
person who holds a first aid certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 271](#1178130-1189814){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178130-1189814.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-110-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must ensure that on board every vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is at least one person who holds a first aid
    certificate and who can immediately render first aid to employees
    who are injured or ill; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for every work place at which employees are working
    on live electrical equipment, there is at least one employee who
    has, in the 12 months before the performance of the work on the
    electrical equipment, successfully completed a course in
    cardiopulmonary resuscitation given by an approved organization.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to shore-based
  employees for whom a first aid room, a health unit or a medical
  facility is provided on shore.

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** Emergency medical and
dental care while employees are on board a vessel or landed in a foreign
port must be provided free of charge.

### [Medical Care Ashore]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756215 .Subheading}

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** Measures must be taken
to ensure that employees have access, when in port, to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} outpatient treatment for injury and illness;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} hospitalization when necessary; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} dental treatment.

### [First Aid Kit, Medicine Chest, Medical Equipment and Medical Guide]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756220 .Subheading}

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must provide and maintain for a work place set out in column
  1 of Table 1 to this section the type of first aid kit set out in
  column 2 and a medical guide.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The first aid kit and its contents, as well as the
  medical equipment and medical guide carried on board a vessel, must be
  properly maintained and inspected at regular intervals of not more
  than 12 months, by a qualified person to ensure that supplies and
  equipment are properly stored and labelled with directions for their
  use and their expiry date, that all equipment functions as required
  and that all supplies have not reached or exceeded their expiry dates
  and are not set to expire before to the next scheduled inspection
  date.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every first aid kit must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} accessible when an employee is on board the vessel;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} clearly identified by a conspicuous sign.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A first aid kit must contain the supplies and
  equipment set out in column 1 of Table 2 to this section in the
  applicable quantities set out in column 2.

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel that is engaged on an
  unlimited voyage, a near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an international
  voyage of more than three days' duration, other than an inland voyage,
  must carry a medicine chest, medical equipment and the most recent
  edition of the International Medical Guide for Ships, published by the
  World Health Organization.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The medicine chest and its contents, as well as the
  medical equipment and the International Medical Guide for Ships must
  be properly maintained and inspected at regular intervals of not more
  than 12 months by a qualified person to ensure that supplies and
  medicines are properly stored and labelled with directions for their
  use and their expiry date, that all equipment functions as required
  and that all supplies and medicines have not reached or exceeded their
  expiry dates and are not set to expire before the next scheduled
  inspection date.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every medicine chest must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} accessible when an employee is on board the vessel;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} clearly identified by a conspicuous sign.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer must provide and maintain a medicine
  chest with supplies and medicine in accordance with the
  recommendations of the most recent version of the International
  Medical Guide for Ships, taking into consideration the particulars of
  the intended voyage.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 272(E)](#1178132-1189816){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178132-1189816.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-115-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 115"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** If a hazard of skin or
eye injury from a hazardous substance exists in a work place, shower
facilities to wash the skin and eye wash facilities to irrigate the eyes
must be provided for immediate use by employees or, if it is not
feasible to do so, portable equipment must be provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 273](#1178134-1189820){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178134-1189820.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-116-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 116"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Transportation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756250 .Subheading}

**[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** Before assigning
employees to a detached work place, the employer must provide them with
the following for that work place:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} suitable means of transporting an injured employee to
  the vessel, a medical facility or a hospital;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who holds a first aid certificate to
  accompany an injured employee and to render first aid in transit if
  required; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a means of communication between the detached work
  place and the vessel.

### [Posting of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756255 .Subheading}

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  employer must post and keep posted in a conspicuous place accessible
  to every employee on board a vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the first aid to be rendered for
    any injury, disabling injury or illness; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information regarding the location of medicine
    chests.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} At a detached work place, the information referred to
  in subsection (1) must be kept inside the first aid kit referred to in
  section 114.

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756261 .Subheading}

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an injured or ill employee reports for first aid to a person in
  accordance with section 110 or if a person who holds a first aid
  certificate renders first aid to an employee, the person must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter in a first aid record the following
    information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of the reporting of the injury,
      disabling injury or illness,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the full name of the injured or ill employee,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the occurrence
      of the injury, disabling injury or illness,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the injury, disabling
      injury or illness,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the first aid rendered, if
      any, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of arrangements made for the
      treatment or transportation of the injured or ill employee; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sign the first aid record beneath the information
    entered in accordance with paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep a first aid record for a
  period of two years after the day on which information is entered in
  it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 34(F)](#1520644-1525291){.wb-lbx

## [PART 7]{.HLabel1}[Hazard Prevention Program]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756273 .Part}

### [Development]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756274 .Subheading}

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** The employer must, in
consultation with and with the participation of the policy committee,
or, if there is no policy committee, the work place committee or the
health and safety representative, develop, implement and monitor a
program for the prevention of hazards, including ergonomics-related
hazards, in the work place that is appropriate to the size of the work
place and the nature of the hazards and that includes the following
components:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an implementation plan;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hazard identification and assessment methodology;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} hazard identification and assessment;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} preventive measures;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} employee training; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a program evaluation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 275(E)](#1178138-1189825){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178138-1189825.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Implementation Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756282 .Subheading}

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} develop an implementation plan that specifies the
    time frame for each phase of the development and implementation of
    the prevention program;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} monitor the progress of the implementation of the
    preventive measures; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} review the time frame of the implementation plan
    regularly and, if necessary, revise it.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In implementing the prevention program, the employer
  must ensure, if feasible, that ergonomics-related hazards are
  identified and assessed and that they are eliminated or reduced, as
  required by subsection 124(1), and that any person assigned to
  identify and assess ergonomics-related hazards has the necessary
  instructions and training.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 276](#1178140-1189828){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178140-1189828.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Hazard Identification and Assessment Methodology]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756289 .Subheading}

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must develop a hazard identification and assessment
  methodology, including an identification and assessment methodology
  for ergonomics-related hazards, taking into account the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any hazardous occurrence investigation reports;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} first aid records and minor injury records;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} work place health protection programs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} results of any work place inspections;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any employee reports made under paragraph 126(1)(g)
    or (h) of the Act or under section 275;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any government or employer reports, studies and
    tests concerning the health and safety of employees;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any reports made under the [Safety and Health
    Committees and Representatives
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-305);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the record of hazardous substances; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information, including
    ergonomics-related information.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The hazard identification and assessment methodology
  must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the steps and time frame for identifying and
    assessing the hazards;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the keeping of a record of the hazards; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a time frame for reviewing and, if necessary,
    revising the methodology.

### [Hazard Identification and Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756305 .Subheading}

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must identify and assess the hazards in the work place,
  including ergonomics-related hazards, in accordance with the
  methodology developed under section 122 and taking into account

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the hazard;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of ergonomics-related hazards, all
    ergonomics-related factors, such as

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the physical demands of the work activities, the
      work environment, the work procedures, the organization of the
      work and the circumstances in which the work activities are
      performed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the characteristics of materials, goods,
      persons, animals, things and workspaces and the features of tools
      and equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employees' level of exposure to the hazard;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the frequency and duration of the employees'
    exposure to the hazard;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the effects, real or apprehended, of the exposure
    on the health and safety of the employee;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the preventive measures in place to address the
    hazard;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any employee reports made under paragraphs
    126(1)(g) or (h) of the Act or under section 275; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must assess a cargo hold in accordance
  with subsection (1) to determine if it is a confined space and each
  time there is a change in conditions that may create a hazard.

### [Preventive Measures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756319 .Subheading}

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must, in order to address identified and assessed hazards,
  including ergonomics-related hazards, take preventive measures to
  address the assessed hazard in the following order of priority:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the elimination of the hazard, including by way of
    engineering controls which may involve mechanical aids, equipment
    design or redesign that take into account the physical attributes of
    the employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reduction of the hazard, including isolating
    it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the provision of personal protection equipment,
    clothing, devices or materials; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} administrative procedures respecting, among other
    things, work permits issued under Part 13, the management of hazard
    exposure and recovery periods and the management of work patterns
    and methods.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} As part of the preventive measures, the employer must
  develop and implement a preventive maintenance program in order to
  avoid failures that could result in a hazard to employees.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that any preventive measure
  must not in itself create a hazard and must take into account the
  effects on the work place.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The preventive measures must include steps to address

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} newly identified hazards in an expeditious manner;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ergonomics-related hazards that are identified when
    planning implementation of change to the work environment or to work
    duties, equipment, practices or processes.

### [Employee Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756331 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 277(E)

\]
:::

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must provide each employee with health and safety training,
  including training relating to ergonomics, which must include the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the hazard prevention program implemented in
    accordance with this Part to prevent hazards applicable to the
    employee, including the hazard identification and assessment
    methodology and the preventive measures taken by the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the work place and the hazards
    associated with it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee's duty to report under paragraphs
    126(1)(g) and (h) of the Act and under section 275; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an overview of the Act and these Regulations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must provide an employee with training

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} when new information in respect of a hazard in the
    work place becomes available to the employer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before the employee is assigned a new activity or
    exposed to a new hazard.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer must review the employee training
  program and, if necessary, revise it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least once every three years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when there is a change in conditions in respect of
    a hazard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when new information in respect of a hazard in the
    work place becomes available to the employer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each time an employee is provided with training, the
  employee must acknowledge in writing that they received it and the
  employer must acknowledge in writing that they provided it.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep, in paper or electronic form,
  records of the training that each employee is provided with for a
  period of two years after the day on which the employee ceases to be
  exposed to a hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 278](#1178144-1189834){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178144-1189834.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 37](#1299355-1300437){.wb-lbx

:::
