Version](section-125-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 125"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Program Evaluation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756347 .Subheading}

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must evaluate the effectiveness of the hazard prevention
  program, including its ergonomics-related components, and, if
  necessary, revise it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least once every three years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when there is a change in conditions in respect of
    a hazard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when new information in respect of a hazard in the
    work place becomes available to the employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The evaluation of the effectiveness of the prevention
  program must be based on the following documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conditions related to the work place and the
    activities of the employees;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any work place inspection reports;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any hazardous occurrence investigation reports;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any safety audits;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} first aid records and any injury statistics,
    including records and statistics relating to ergonomics-related
    first aid and injuries;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any observations of the policy and work place
    committees, or the health and safety representative, on the
    effectiveness of the prevention program; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 279(F)](#1178153-1189841){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178153-1189841.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Reports and Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756361 .Subheading}

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  program evaluation has been conducted under section 126, the employer
  must prepare a program evaluation report.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep every program evaluation
  report for a period of six years after the day on which the report is
  prepared and make it readily available for examination for that
  period.

## [PART 8]{.HLabel1}[Diving Operations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756365 .Part}

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** The employer must
ensure that all diving operations from a vessel meet the same
requirements as those set out in Part XVIII of the [Canada Occupational
Health and Safety Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-304).

## [PART 9]{.HLabel1}[Persons Transfer Apparatus]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756367 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756368 .Subheading}

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[boatswain's chair]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[boatswain's chair]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a single-point
    adjustable suspension scaffold consisting of a seat or sling
    designed to accommodate one employee in a sitting position. ([chaise
    de gabier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[persons transfer apparatus]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[persons transfer apparatus]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a
    platform, bucket, landing boom or basket or boatswain's chair
    designed specifically for the purpose of transporting, lifting,
    moving and positioning persons. ([appareil de transbordement de
    personnes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756372 .Subheading}

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to
every persons transfer apparatus used for the purpose of carrying
persons on board a vessel, and every safety device attached to that
apparatus.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 280(F)](#1178155-1189843){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178155-1189843.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-130-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 130"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Standards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756374 .Subheading}

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every persons transfer apparatus and
  every safety device attached to it must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if feasible, meet the standards set out in the
    applicable standard referred to in subsection (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be used, operated and maintained in accordance with
    the standards set out in the applicable standard referred to in
    subsection (2).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the applicable
  standard for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} elevators, dumbwaiters and escalators is ASME
    A17.1-2007/CSA B44-07, Safety Code for Elevators and Escalators, and
    CSA Standard CAN/CSA-B44.1/ASME-A17.5-04, Elevator and Escalator
    Electrical Equipment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} manlifts is CSA Standard CAN/CSA-B311-02, Safety
    Code for Manlifts;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} personnel hoists is CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z185-M87
    (R2006), Safety Code for Personnel Hoists;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} lifts for persons with physical disabilities is CSA
    Standard CAN/CSA B355-00 (R2005), Lifts for Persons with Physical
    Disabilities;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} descent control devices is CSA Standard Z259.3-99
    (R2004), Descent Control Devices; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} landing booms is the most recent edition of The
    Seaway Handbook, issued by St. Lawrence Seaway Management
    Corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 281(E)](#1178157-1189845){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178157-1189845.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-131-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 131"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Use and Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756386 .Subheading}

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  persons transfer apparatus on a vessel must not be used or operated in
  the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with a load in excess of the load that it was
    designed and installed to move safely; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the roll of the vessel is more than the
    maximum roll for the safe operation of the persons transfer
    apparatus recommended by the manufacturer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A persons transfer apparatus must not be used or
  operated while any safety device attached to it is inoperative.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A safety device attached to a persons transfer
  apparatus must not be altered, interfered with or rendered
  inoperative.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) and (3) do not apply in respect of a
  persons transfer apparatus or a safety device that is being inspected,
  tested, repaired or maintained by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 38(E)](#1299357-1300440){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299357-1300440.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-132-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 132"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  persons transfer apparatus must not be used to transfer freight unless
  the apparatus is specifically designed for freight and people or if
  there is an emergency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every transfer of a person by means of a persons
  transfer apparatus must be made only when visibility and environmental
  conditions are such that the transfer can be made safely.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person is transferred by means of a persons
  transfer apparatus from one place to another on the vessel or from the
  vessel to another place off a vessel or vice versa,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person in charge of the transfer operation must
    be in direct radio contact with the operator of the apparatus; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person to be transferred must

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be instructed in the safety procedures to be
      followed by them,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} wear a flotation suit if the conditions warrant,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} must use a life jacket or a personal flotation
      device unless, if for medical or emergency reasons, it is not
      feasible to wear one.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a person is transferred by a basket to or from a
  drilling unit or an offshore production facility, the drilling unit or
  production facility must be equipped with at least two buoyant
  baskets.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (4),
  [[basket]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[drilling
  unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[offshore]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and
  [[production facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} have the same meaning as
  in section 1.1 of the [Oil and Gas Occupational Safety and Health
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-87-612).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 282](#1178159-1189848){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178159-1189848.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 39](#1299359-1300442){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299359-1300442.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-133-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 133"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Boatswain's Chair]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756404 .Subheading}

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  boatswain's chair must provide stable and adequate support for the
  user.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A boatswain's chair must be suspended from a parapet
  clamp, cornice hook, thrust-out beam or other solid anchorage having a
  working load limit that is at least equivalent to that of the
  suspension system for the boatswain's chair.

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** A boatswain's chair
must only be used when visibility and weather conditions permit its safe
use.

### [Inspection and Testing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756409 .Subheading}

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every persons transfer apparatus
  must be inspected and found to be in serviceable condition before each
  use and all ropes, wires or other vital parts that show signs of
  significant wear must be replaced before the apparatus is used.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every persons transfer apparatus and every safety
  device attached to it must be inspected and tested by a qualified
  person to ascertain that the requirements referred to in section 131
  are met

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the persons transfer apparatus and any
    safety device attached to it are operated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after an alteration to the persons transfer
    apparatus or any safety device attached to it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} once every 12 months.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A record of each inspection and test made in
  accordance with subsection (2) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be made and signed by the qualified person who made
    the inspection and performed the test;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include the date of the inspection and test and the
    identification and location of the persons transfer apparatus and
    any safety devices that were inspected and tested;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the observations of the qualified person on
    the safety of the apparatus and any safety device;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be kept by the employer on the vessel on which the
    persons transfer apparatus is located for a period of two years
    after the day on which the inspection is signed; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} be made readily available for examination by the
    operator of the apparatus.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 40(E)](#1299361-1300445){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299361-1300445.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-136-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 136"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Repair and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756422 .Subheading}

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** Repair and maintenance
of a persons transfer apparatus or a safety device attached to it must
be performed by a qualified person appointed by the employer.

## [PART 10]{.HLabel1}[Protection Equipment]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756424 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756425 .Subheading}

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must ensure that every person granted access to a work place
  who is exposed to a health or safety hazard uses the protection
  equipment prescribed by this Part

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is not feasible to eliminate a health or
    safety hazard in a work place or to control it within safe limits;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the use of the protection equipment may prevent
    or reduce injury from that hazard.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All protection equipment must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be designed to protect the person from the hazard
    for which it is provided;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not in itself create a hazard;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be maintained, inspected and tested by a qualified
    person; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if necessary to prevent a health hazard, be
    maintained in a clean and sanitary condition by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 283](#1178166-1189852){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178166-1189852.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-138-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 138"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Protective Headwear]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756435 .Subheading}

**[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** If there is a hazard of
head injury in a work place, the employer must provide protective
headwear that meets the standards set out in CSA Standard
CAN/CSA-Z94.1-05, Industrial Protective Headwear - Performance,
Selection, Care, and Use.

### [Protective Footwear]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756437 .Subheading}

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  there is a hazard of a foot injury or electric shock through footwear
  in a work place, the employer must ensure that every person granted
  access to that work place uses protective footwear that meets the
  standards set out in CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z195-02 (R2008), Protective
  Footwear.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is a hazard of slipping in a work place,
  non-slip footwear must be used.

### [Eye and Face Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756441 .Subheading}

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** If there is a hazard of
injury in a work place to the eyes, face, ears or front of the neck, the
employer must provide eye or face protectors that meet the standards set
out in CSA Standard Z94.3-07, Eye and Face Protectors.

### [Respiratory Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756443 .Subheading}

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  there is a risk of injury or disease due to exposure to an oxygen
  deficient atmosphere in a work place, the employer must provide every
  person who is granted access to the work place with respiratory
  protective equipment or a breathing apparatus by means of which air
  will be supplied and that meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is listed in the NIOSH publication entitled
    Certified Equipment List, as amended from time to time; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it protects the respiratory tract against the
    oxygen deficiency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury or disease due to
  exposure to an airborne hazardous substance in a work place, the
  employer shall provide every person who is granted access to the work
  place with respiratory protective equipment or a breathing apparatus
  that meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if air is supplied by means of the respiratory
    protective equipment or breathing apparatus, it is listed in the
    NIOSH publication entitled Certified Equipment List, as amended from
    time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if air is not supplied by means of the respiratory
    protective equipment or breathing apparatus, it is listed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the NIOSH publication entitled Certified
      Equipment List, as amended from time to time, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the CSA Group publication entitled CSA Group
      Product Listing, as amended from time to time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for respiratory protective equipment or a breathing
    apparatus referred to in subparagraph (b)(ii), it meets the
    requirements set out in CSA Standard Z94.4.1, Performance of
    filtering respirators; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it protects the respiratory tract against the
    hazardous substance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The respiratory protective equipment or breathing
  apparatus referred to in subsection (1) or (2) must be selected,
  fitted, cared for, used and maintained in accordance with CSA Standard
  Z94.4, Selection, use, and care of respirators.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that the air that is
  supplied by means of respiratory protective equipment or breathing
  apparatus referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (2)(a) and the system
  that supplies the air, including its cylinders, meet the requirements
  set out in CSA Standard Z180.1, Compressed breathing air and systems.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-140, s. 4](#1301895-1304742){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1301895-1304742.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 13](#1343864-1344413){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343864-1344413.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20220222.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[142.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142.1 .sectionLabel}** If a steel or
aluminum self-contained breathing apparatus cylinder has a dent deeper
than 1.5 mm and less than 50 mm in major diameter or shows evidence of
deep isolated pitting, cracks or splits, the cylinder must be removed
from service until it has been shown to be safe for use by means of a
hydrostatic test at a pressure equal to one and one-half times the
maximum allowable working pressure.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 13](#1343864-1344429){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343864-1344429.html"}
:::

### [Skin Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756451 .Subheading}

**[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** If there is a hazard of
injury or disease to or through the skin of a person in a work place,
the employer must provide any person granted access to the work place
with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a shield or a screen;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a cream or a barrier lotion to protect the skin; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an appropriate body covering.

### [Fall-protection Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756456 .Subheading}

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must provide a fall-protection system to every person, other
  than an employee who is installing or removing a fall-protection
  system, who is granted access to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an unguarded work area that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 2.4 m above the nearest permanent safe
      level,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} above any moving parts of machinery or any other
      surface or thing that could cause injury to a person on contact,
      or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} above an open hold;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a structure referred to in Part 2 that is more than
    3 m above a permanent safe level; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a ladder at a height of more than 2.4 m above the
    nearest permanent safe level and because of the nature of the work,
    that person can use only one hand to hold onto the ladder.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The components of a fall-protection system must meet
  the following standards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.1-05, Body Belts and Saddles for
    Work Positioning and Travel Restraint;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z259.2.1-98 (R2008), Fall
    Arresters, Vertical Lifelines and Rails;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z259.2.2-98 (R2009),
    Self-Retracting Devices for Personal Fall-Arrest Systems;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.2.3-99 (R2004), Descent Control
    Devices;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.10-06, Full Body Harnesses;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.11-05, Energy Absorbers and
    Lanyards;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z259.12-01 (R2006), Connecting
    Components for Personal Fall Arrest Systems (PFAS);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.13-04 (R2009), Flexible
    Horizontal Lifeline Systems; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.16-04 (R2009), Design of Active
    Fall-Protection Systems.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The anchor of a fall-protection system must be
  capable of withstanding a force of 17.8 kN.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A fall-protection system that is used to arrest the
  fall of a person must prevent that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from being subjected to a peak fall arrest force of
    more than 8 kN; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} from falling freely for more than 1.2 m.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employer must train and instruct every employee
  required to install or remove a fall-protection system in a work place
  in the procedures to be followed for the installation or removal of
  the system.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 284(F)](#1178168-1189855){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178168-1189855.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-144-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 144"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Loose Clothing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756480 .Subheading}

**[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** Loose clothing, long
hair, dangling accessories, jewellery or other similar items that may be
hazardous to the health or safety of an employee in a work place must
not be worn unless they are tied, covered or otherwise secured as to
prevent the hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 285](#1178173-1189858){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178173-1189858.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-145-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 145"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Protection Against Moving Vehicles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756482 .Subheading}

**[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** If persons granted
access to a work place are regularly exposed to the risk of coming into
contact with moving vehicles during their work, the employer must ensure
that they wear a high-visibility vest or other similar clothing that is
readily visible under all conditions of use or that they are protected
by a barricade that is readily visible under all conditions of use.

### [Protection Against Drowning]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756484 .Subheading}

- **[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  in a work place, there is a hazard of drowning, as a result of work
  activities, other than vessel abandonment drills, the employer must
  provide every person granted access to the work place with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a life jacket or other flotation device that meets
    the buoyancy standards set out in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian General Standards Board (CGSB)
      Standard CAN/CGSB-65.7-2007, Life Jackets; or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} Regulation 2 of Part 1 to IMO Resolution
      MSC.81(70), adopted on December 11, 1998 and entitled Revised
      Recommendation on Testing of Life-Saving Appliances; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a safety net or a fall-protection system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, in a work place, there is a hazard of drowning,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} emergency equipment must be provided and held in
    readiness;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a qualified person to operate all the emergency
    equipment provided must be present and ready to intervene;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if appropriate, a vessel must be provided and held
    in readiness; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} written emergency procedures must be prepared by
    the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 41](#1299363-1300448){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299363-1300448.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-147-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 147"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756496 .Subheading}

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  record of all self-contained breathing apparatus provided by the
  employer must be kept by the employer on the vessel on which the
  breathing apparatus is located for the duration of its use and must
  remain on the vessel on which the breathing apparatus was located for
  a period of two years after the day on which the apparatus ceases to
  be used.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record must contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the breathing apparatus and the
    date of its acquisition by the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and result of each inspection and test of
    the breathing apparatus;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date and nature of any maintenance work
    performed on the breathing apparatus since its acquisition by the
    employer; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who performed the
    inspection, test or maintenance of the breathing apparatus.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 42](#1299365-1300451){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299365-1300451.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-148-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 148"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Training and Instruction]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756504 .Subheading}

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer must instruct every person granted access to a work place who
  is to use protection equipment in the use of the equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must train and instruct every employee
  who uses protection equipment in the use, operation and maintenance of
  the equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The instruction referred to in subsections (1)
  and (2) must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out in writing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} kept by the employer and made readily available for
    examination by every person granted access to the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 286(F)](#1178175-1189860){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178175-1189860.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-149-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 149"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Defective Protection Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756511 .Subheading}

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an employee finds any defect in protection equipment that may render
  it unsafe for use, they must report the defect to their employer as
  soon as feasible.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must mark or tag as unsafe and remove
  from service any protection equipment used by employees that has a
  defect that may render it unsafe for use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 287](#1178177-1189862){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178177-1189862.html"}
:::

## [PART 12]{.HLabel1}[Prevention of Noise and Vibration]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756574 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756575 .Subheading}

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** Subsection 161(5) does
not apply in respect of vessels constructed before the day on which the
MLC 2006 came into force in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 419](#1431202-1435336){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431202-1435336.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-158-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 158"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756579 .Subheading}

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[A-weighted sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[A-weighted sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    sound pressure level as determined by a measurement system which
    includes an A-weighting filter that meets the requirements set out
    in the International Electrotechnical Commission International
    Standard IEC 61672-1:2002(E), 1^st^ edition 2002-2005
    Electroacoustics -- Sound Level Meters. ([niveau de pression
    acoustique pondérée A]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dBA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dBA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means decibel A-weighted and is a unit
    of A-weighted sound pressure level. ([dBA]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[noise exposure level (L~ex,8~)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[noise exposure level (L~ex,8~)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means 10
    times the logarithm to the base 10 of the time integral over any
    24-hour period of a squared A-weighted sound pressure divided by 8,
    the reference sound pressure being 20 µPa. ([niveau d'exposition
    (L~ex,8~)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sound level meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sound level meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an instrument for
    measuring levels of sound and impulse sound that meets the standards
    set out in CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z107.56-06, Procedures for the
    Measurement of Occupational Noise
    Exposure. ([sonomètre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means 20 times the
    logarithm to the base 10 of the ratio of the root mean square
    pressure of a sound to the reference sound pressure of 20 µPa,
    expressed in decibels. ([niveau de pression
    acoustique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

**[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 420\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-160-20210604.html "Link to previous version of section 160"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Levels of Sound]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756593 .Subheading}

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4),
  the level of sound in a work place must be less than 85 dB.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), if it is not feasible for
  an employer to maintain the sound level in the work place at less than
  85 dB, an employee must not be exposed in any 24-hour period

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to a level of sound set out in column 1 of the
    table to this section for a number of hours that is more than the
    number set out in column 2; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to any combination of the different levels of sound
    set out in column 1 of the table to this section, if the number of
    hours of exposure to each level of sound divided by the maximum
    number of hours of exposure for that level per 24-hour period set
    out in column 2 of the table to this section is more than one.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee must not be exposed to a continuous level
  of sound in crew accommodation that is more than 75 dB.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the level of impulse sound in a work place is more
  than 140 dB, the employer must provide every employee entering the
  work place with a hearing protector that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the standards set out in CSA Standard
    CAN/CSA-Z94.2-02 (R2007), Hearing Protection Devices ---
    Performance, Selection, Care and Use; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reduces the peak level of impulse sound reaching
    the employee's ears to 140 dB or less.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise specified, the limits for noise
  levels for working spaces and crew accommodation must fall within the
  ILO international guidelines on exposure levels, including those in
  the ILO code of practice entitled Ambient factors in the workplace,
  2001, and, if applicable, the specific protection recommended by the
  IMO, and any subsequent amending and supplementary instruments for
  acceptable noise levels on board vessels.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the documents in English and in French
  referred to in subsection (5) must be kept on board the vessel and be
  made available to employees.

### [Hazard Investigation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756612 .Subheading}

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  it is not feasible for an employer to maintain an employee's exposure
  to a sound level at or below the levels referred to in section 161,
  the employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a qualified person to carry out an
    investigation of the degree of exposure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify the work place committee or the health and
    safety representative of the investigation and of the name of the
    person appointed to carry out the investigation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide every employee entering the work place with
    a hearing protector that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} meets the standards set out in CSA Standard
      CAN/CSA-Z94.2-02 (R2007), Hearing Protection Devices, Performance,
      Selection, Care and Use, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} reduces the level of sound reaching the
      employee's ears to less than 85 dB.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the measurement
  of the A-weighted sound pressure level in a work place must be
  performed instantaneously, in normal working conditions, using the
  slow response setting of a sound level meter.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} During the investigation referred to in subsection
  (1), the following matters must be considered:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the sources of sound in the work place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the A-weighted sound pressure levels to which the
    employee is likely to be exposed and the duration of that exposure;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the methods being used to reduce the exposure;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the exposure of the employee is likely to
    be more than the limits prescribed by section 161; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the employee is likely to be exposed to a
    noise exposure level (L~ex,8~) equal to or greater than 85 dBA.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the investigation and after
  consultation with the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative, as the case may be, the person appointed to carry out
  the investigation must set out in a written report signed and dated by
  the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} observations respecting the matters considered
    under subsection (3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} recommendations respecting the measures that are to
    be taken in order to comply with section 161; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} recommendations respecting the use of hearing
    protectors by employees who are exposed to a noise exposure level
    (L~ex,8~) equal to or greater than 85 dBA and not greater than 87
    dBA.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The report must be kept by the employer at the work
  place where it applies for a period of 10 years after the day on which
  the report is submitted.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} If it is stated in the report that employees are
  likely to be exposed to a noise exposure level (L~ex,8~) equal to or
  greater than 85 dBA, the employer must, without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} post and keep posted a copy of the report in a
    conspicuous place in the work place where it applies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the employees with written information
    describing the hazards associated with exposure to high levels of
    sound.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 291](#1178190-1189876){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178190-1189876.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-162-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 162"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Sound Level Measurement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756635 .Subheading}

**[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** The levels of sound
must be measured by using the slow exponential-time-averaging
characteristic and the A-weighting characteristic of a sound level
meter.

### [Warning Signs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756637 .Subheading}

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** In a work place where
the level of sound is 85 dB or more, the employer must post signs
warning persons entering the work place

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that there is a hazardous level of sound in the work
  place;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, of the maximum number of hours of
  exposure determined under subsection 161(2); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, of the requirement to wear a hearing
  protector.

## [PART 13]{.HLabel1}[Work Permit]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756642 .Part}

### [Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756643 .Subheading}

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** The employer must
assess the following types of work to determine if the work presents a
hazard that is capable of causing death or serious injury:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} work on live electrical equipment that cannot be
  isolated or grounded;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} work on electrical equipment that is capable of
  becoming live during that work;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} hot work, as defined in section 189;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} work that requires exposure to hazardous substances
  beyond the limits referred to in subsection 255(1); and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other work that may present a hazard that is
  capable of causing death or serious injury.

### [Issuance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756650 .Subheading}

**[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** The employer is
required to issue a written work permit to a qualified person before the
commencement of the following types of work:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} work that requires entry into confined spaces; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other work that has been assessed under section
  165 as presenting a hazard that is capable of causing death or serious
  injury.

### [Contents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756654 .Subheading}

**[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** The work permit must
contain the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who issues the permit;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person to whom it is issued;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the periods during which the permit is valid;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the type of work to be performed and its location;
  and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} assessment of conditions related to the hazard of
  performing the work, and instructions arising from those conditions,
  including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the work procedures to be followed,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the identification of equipment that is to be
    locked out in accordance with CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z460-05, Control
    of Hazardous Energy - Lockout and Other Methods,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a description of any safety tests to be performed
    before the work is performed, during the performance of the work and
    following the completion of the work,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the specification of the particulars of the tags
    or signs to be used, if any,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the specification of the protection equipment to be
    used, if any,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an emergency, the procedures to be
    followed,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a description of the specific space, work or
    electrical equipment to which the instructions apply, and

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the identification of any other work permit that
    may affect the emergency or work procedures to be followed.

### [Requirements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756669 .Subheading}

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  work permit must be signed by the employer and the terms, rights and
  obligations explained to and signed by the person to whom that permit
  is issued.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The work permit must be made readily available for
  examination by employees for the period in which the work is being
  performed and it must be kept by the employer for a period of two
  years after the day on which the work was completed at the place of
  business nearest to the work place in which the work was completed.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Work authorized under a work permit may be performed
  only after the equipment has been locked out in accordance with the
  standard referred to in subparagraph 167(e)(ii).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 46](#1299378-1300462){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299378-1300462.html"}
:::

:::

## [PART 14]{.HLabel1}[Confined Spaces]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756674 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756675 .Subheading}

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** A person must not enter
a confined space without having been issued a work permit under section
166.

**[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2021-122, s. 47\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-170-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 170"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Assessment of Condition]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756678 .Subheading}

- **[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before authorizing a person to enter
  a confined space on a vessel, the employer must appoint a marine
  chemist or other qualified person to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carry out an assessment of any hazardous substance
    in the confined space; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify the necessary tests to determine whether
    employees are likely to be exposed to the hazard.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the context of the assessment, the marine chemist
  or other qualified person must, at a minimum, verify that the
  following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of any chemical agent to which
    the person is likely to be exposed in the confined space is not more
    than the value or level referred to in subsection 255(1) or the
    percentage referred to in subsection 255(5);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of airborne hazardous substances,
    other than chemical agents, in the confined space is not hazardous
    to the health or safety of the person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the percentage of oxygen in the atmosphere in the
    confined space is not less than 19.5 per cent by volume and not more
    than 23 per cent by volume at normal atmospheric pressure and in any
    case the partial pressure of oxygen is not less than 148 mm Hg; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the value, level or percentage referred to in
    paragraphs (a) to (c) can be maintained during the period of
    proposed occupancy of the confined space by the person.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The marine chemist or other qualified person must, in
  a written report signed by that person, set out the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the vessel on which the confined space
    is located;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the location of the confined space on the vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a record of the results of the assessment made in
    accordance with subsection (2);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the type, model, serial number and date of last
    calibration of any instrument used in the assessment process;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an evaluation of the hazards of the confined space;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has established procedures to be
    followed by a person entering into, exiting from or performing work
    in a confined space, which of those procedures are applicable;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has not established procedures
    referred to in paragraph (f), the procedures to be followed by the
    person referred to in that paragraph;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the protection equipment referred to in Part 10
    that is to be used by every person granted access to the confined
    space; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has established emergency
    procedures to be followed in the event of an accident or other
    emergency in or near the confined space, which of the procedures are
    to be followed, including immediate evacuation of the confined space
    when

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an alarm is activated, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} there is any significant change in the value,
      level or percentage referred to in subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 292](#1178197-1189879){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178197-1189879.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-171-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 171"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** The written report
referred to in subsection 171(3) must be kept by the employer on the
vessel on which the confined space is located for a period of two years
after the day on which the marine chemist or other qualified person
signs the report.

### [Additional Requirements for the Issuance of a Work Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756701 .Subheading}

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to the requirements set out in section 168, before an
  employer issues a work permit under section 166 in respect of work
  that requires entry into a confined space, the employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain a written report referred to in subsection
    171(3) from a qualified person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that any liquid in which a person may drown
    or any free-flowing solid in which a person may become entrapped has
    been removed from the confined space;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the entry of any liquid, free-flowing
    solid or hazardous substance into the confined space has been
    prevented by a secure means of disconnection or the fitting of blank
    flanges;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all electrical equipment and mechanical
    equipment that presents a hazard to a person entering into, exiting
    from or performing work in the confined space has been disconnected
    from its power source and locked out in accordance with the standard
    referred to in subparagraph 167(e)(ii);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the opening for entry into and exit
    from the confined space is sufficient in size to allow safe passage
    of a person who is using protection equipment; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} establish an entry control system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The written report referred to in subsection 171(3)
  and any procedures identified in that report must be explained to a
  person who is about to enter into the confined space and that person
  must sign a dated copy of the report indicating that they have read
  the report and that the report and the procedures were explained to
  them.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If conditions in the confined space or the nature of
  the work to be performed in the confined space are such that any of
  paragraphs 171(2)(a) to (c) or (1)(b) to (e) cannot be complied with,
  the employer must at a minimum ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a qualified person is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in attendance outside the confined space,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in communication with the person inside the
      confined space, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} provided with a suitable alarm device for
      summoning assistance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each person granted access to the confined space
    must wear a safety harness that is securely attached to a lifeline
    that is attached to a secure anchor outside the confined space and
    is controlled by the qualified person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} two or more employees, which may include the
    qualified person, are in the immediate vicinity of the confined
    space to assist in the event of an accident or other emergency; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} one of the employees referred to in paragraph (c)
    is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} trained in the emergency procedures referred to
      in paragraph 171(3)(i),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the holder of a first aid certificate, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} provided with the protection equipment referred
      to in paragraph 171(3)(h) and any emergency equipment required by
      the procedures established by the employer under paragraph
      171(3)(i).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person must not close off a confined spaced until a
  qualified person has verified that no person is inside it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 293](#1178208-1189884){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178208-1189884.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 48](#1299381-1300465){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299381-1300465.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-173-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 173"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Ventilation Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756723 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 294(F)

\]
:::

- **[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  hazardous substance may be produced in a confined space by the work to
  be performed,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the confined space must be ventilated in accordance
    with subsection (2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each person granted access to the confined space
    must use respiratory protective equipment or a breathing apparatus
    referred to in section 142.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an airborne hazardous substance or oxygen in the
  atmosphere in a confined space is maintained at the value, level or
  percentage referred to in subsection 171(2) by the use of ventilation
  equipment, a person may only be granted access to the confined space
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ventilation equipment is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} equipped with an alarm that will, if the
      equipment fails, be activated automatically and be audible or
      visible to any person in the confined space, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} monitored by an employee who is in constant
      attendance at the equipment and in communication with any person
      in the confined space; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the event of failure of the ventilation
    equipment, sufficient time will be available for the person to
    escape from the confined space before one of the following occurs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} their exposure to or the concentration of a
      hazardous substance in the confined space is more than the value,
      level or percentage prescribed in paragraph 171(2)(a) or (b), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the percentage of oxygen in the atmosphere
      ceases to meet the requirements of paragraph 171(2)(c).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employee referred to in subparagraph (2)(a)(ii)
  must activate an alarm in the event of faulty operation of the
  ventilation equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 295](#1178221-1189893){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178221-1189893.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 14](#1343866-1344430){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343866-1344430.html"}
:::
