[Previous
Version](section-174-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 174"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 15]{.HLabel1}[Electrical Safety]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756736 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756737 .Subheading}

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[control device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[control device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device that will
    safely disconnect electrical equipment from its source of
    energy. ([dispositif de commande]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[direct supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[direct supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means on-site, in-view
    observation and guidance by a qualified person while an employee
    performs an assigned task. ([supervision immédiate]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[guarantor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[guarantor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who gives a
    guarantee of isolation. ([garant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[guarded]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[guarded]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means in respect of electrical
    equipment, covered, shielded, fenced, enclosed or otherwise
    protected by means of suitable covers or casing, barriers,
    guardrails, screens, mats or platforms to remove the possibility of
    dangerous contact or approach by persons or
    objects. ([protégé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[person in charge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[person in charge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person
    who supervises employees performing work on or a live test of
    isolated electrical equipment. ([responsable]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

### [Safety Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756744 .Subheading}

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  testing or work performed on electrical equipment must be performed by
  a person in charge or an employee under the direct supervision of a
  person in charge.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before authorizing a person to test or perform work
  on electrical equipment, the employer must ensure that a work permit
  referred to in section 166 has been obtained.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk that the person in charge or the
  employee under the direct supervision of the person in charge could
  receive a hazardous electrical shock during the performance of their
  testing or work,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person in charge or the employee must use
    insulated protection equipment and tools that will protect them from
    injury; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee must be trained and instructed in the
    use of the insulated protection equipment and tools.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), if an employee is working
  on or near electrical equipment that is live or may become live, the
  electrical equipment must be guarded.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible for the electrical equipment to
  be guarded, the employer must take measures to protect the employee
  from injury by insulating the equipment from the employee or the
  employee from the ground.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 296](#1178234-1189898){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178234-1189898.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-176-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 176"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Safety Watcher]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756753 .Subheading}

- **[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an employee is working on or near live electrical equipment and,
  because of the nature of the work or the condition or location of the
  work place, it is necessary for the safety of the employee that the
  work be observed by a person not engaged in the work, the employer
  must appoint a safety watcher

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to warn all employees in the work place of the
    hazard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that all safety precautions and
    procedures are complied with.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Safety watchers must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} informed of their duties as safety watchers and of
    the hazard involved in the work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} trained and instructed in the procedures to follow
    in the event of an emergency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorized to stop immediately any part of the work
    that they consider dangerous; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} free of any other duties that might interfere with
    their duties as safety watchers.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), employers may
  appoint themselves as safety watchers.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 297(F)](#1178241-1189902){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178241-1189902.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-177-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 177"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Coordination of Work]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756764 .Subheading}

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** If an employee is
working on or in connection with electrical equipment, that employee and
every other person who is so working, including every safety watcher,
must be fully informed by the employer with respect to the safe
coordination of their work.

### [Switches and Control Devices]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756766 .Subheading}

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s.
  421\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The path of access to every electrical switch,
  control device or meter must be free from obstruction.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an electrical switch or other control device
  controlling the supply of electrical energy to electrical equipment is
  operated only by a person authorized to do so by the employer, the
  switch or control device must be fitted with a locking device that
  only the authorized person can activate.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Control switches for all electrically operated
  machinery must be clearly marked to indicate the switch positions.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 421](#1431205-1435350){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431205-1435350.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Defective Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756772 .Subheading}

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** Defective electrical
equipment must be disconnected from its power source by a means other
than the control switch and notices must be placed on the equipment and
at the control switch to indicate that the equipment is defective.

### [Electrical Fuses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756774 .Subheading}

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Electrical fuses must be of the
  correct ampere rating and fault capacity rating for the circuit in
  which they are installed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee must not replace missing or burnt-out
  fuses unless authorized to do so by a person in charge.

### [Power Supply Cables]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756778 .Subheading}

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Power supply cables for portable
  electrical equipment must be placed clear of areas used for vehicles
  unless the cables are guarded.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A three-wire power supply cable on electrical
  equipment or appliances must not be altered or changed for the purpose
  of connecting the equipment or appliances to a two-wire power supply.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 298(E)](#1178243-1189904){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178243-1189904.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-182-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 182"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Grounded Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756782 .Subheading}

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** Grounded electrical
equipment and appliances must be used only when connected to a matching
grounded electrical outlet receptacle.

### [Additional Requirement for the Issuance of a Work Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756784 .Subheading}

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** In addition to the
requirements set out in section 168, before an employer issues a work
permit for electrical equipment under section 166, the employer must
provide a guarantee of isolation in respect of each source of electrical
energy.

### [Guarantees of Isolation of Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756786 .Subheading}

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee must not give or receive a guarantee of isolation for
  electrical equipment unless they are authorized in writing by their
  employer to give or receive a guarantee of isolation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No more than one employee is to give a guarantee of
  isolation for a piece of electrical equipment for a given period.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before an employer issues a work permit, the person
  in charge must receive from the guarantor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written guarantee of isolation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if owing to an emergency it is not feasible for the
    person in charge to receive a written guarantee of isolation, a
    non-written guarantee of isolation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The written guarantee of isolation referred to in
  paragraph (3)(a) must be signed by the guarantor and by the person in
  charge and must contain the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the guarantee of isolation
    is given to the person in charge;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the electrical equipment
    will become isolated;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the isolation will be
    terminated, if known;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the procedures by which isolation is assured;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the guarantor and the person in charge;
    and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a statement as to whether live tests are to be
    performed.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a non-written guarantee of isolation referred to
  in paragraph (3)(b) is given, a written record of the guarantee must,
  as soon as feasible, be made by the guarantor and signed by the person
  in charge.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A written record must contain the information
  referred to in subsection (4).

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every written guarantee of isolation and every
  written record must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} kept by the person in charge and made readily
    available for examination by the employee performing the work or
    live test until the work or live test is completed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} given to the employer when the work or live test is
    completed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} kept by the employer at their place of business
    nearest to the work place in which the electrical equipment was
    located when it was isolated, for a period of one year after the
    completion of the work or live test.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a written guarantee of isolation or a written
  record of an oral guarantee of isolation is given to a person in
  charge and the person in charge is replaced at the work place by
  another person in charge before the guarantee has terminated, the
  other person in charge must sign the written guarantee of isolation or
  written record of the oral guarantee of isolation.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} Before an employee gives a guarantee of isolation for
  electrical equipment that obtains all or any portion of its electrical
  energy from a source that is not under the employee's direct control,
  the employee must obtain a guarantee of isolation in respect of the
  source from the person who is in direct control of the source and is
  authorized to give the guarantee in respect of that source.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 299](#1178245-1189907){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178245-1189907.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 49](#1299383-1300467){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299383-1300467.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-185-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 185"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Live Test]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756808 .Subheading}

- **[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee must not give a guarantee of isolation for the performance of
  a live test on isolated electrical equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any other guarantee of isolation given in respect
    of the electrical equipment for any part of the period for which the
    guarantee of isolation is given is terminated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every person to whom the other guarantee of
    isolation referred to in paragraph (a) was given has been informed
    of its termination; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any live test to be performed on the electrical
    equipment will not be hazardous to the health or safety of the
    person performing the live test.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person performing a live test must warn all
  persons who, during or as a result of the test, are likely to be
  exposed to a hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 300(F)](#1178250-1189911){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178250-1189911.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-186-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 186"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Termination of the Guarantee of Isolation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756815 .Subheading}

- **[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  guarantee of isolation ends when work on, or a live test of, isolated
  electrical equipment is completed and the following conditions are
  met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every person in charge

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has informed the guarantor that the work or live
      test is completed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has made and signed a record containing the date
      and hour when they informed the guarantor and the name of the
      guarantor; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on receipt of the information referred to in
    paragraph (a), the guarantor made and signed a record containing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the work or live test was
      completed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person in charge.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The records referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(ii)
  and paragraph (1)(b) must be kept by the employer at the employer's
  place of business nearest to the work place in which the electrical
  equipment was located when it was isolated, for a period of one year
  after the day on which they are signed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 301(E)](#1178252-1189913){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178252-1189913.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 50](#1299385-1300477){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299385-1300477.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-187-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 187"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Safety Grounding]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756824 .Subheading}

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee must not attach a safety ground to electrical equipment
  unless they have tested the electrical equipment and have established
  that it is isolated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of
  electrical equipment that is grounded by means of a grounding switch
  that is an integral part of the equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Work must not be performed on any electrical
  equipment in an area if the following devices are found unless the
  device is connected to a common grounding network:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a grounding bus;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a station grounding network;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a neutral conductor;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} temporary phase grounding; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a metal structure.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, after the connections are made, a safety ground
  is required to ensure the safety of an employee working on the
  electrical equipment, the safety ground must be connected to the
  common grounding network.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every conducting part of a safety ground on isolated
  electrical equipment must have sufficient current carrying capacity to
  conduct the maximum current that is likely to be carried on any part
  of the equipment for the time that is necessary to permit operation of
  any device that is installed on the electrical equipment so that, in
  the event of a short circuit or other electrical current overload, the
  electrical equipment is automatically disconnected from its source of
  electrical energy.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A safety ground must not be attached to or
  disconnected from isolated electrical equipment except in accordance
  with the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground must, if feasible, be attached to
    the pole, structure, apparatus or other thing to which the
    electrical equipment is attached;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all isolated conductors, neutral conductors and
    non-insulated surfaces of the electrical equipment must be
    short-circuited, electrically bonded together and attached by a
    safety ground to a point of safety grounding in a manner that
    establishes equal voltage on all surfaces that can be touched by
    persons who work on the electrical equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground must be attached by means of
    mechanical clamps that are tightened securely and are in direct
    contact with bare metal;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground must be secured so that none of
    its parts can accidentally make contact with any live electrical
    equipment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground must be attached and disconnected
    using insulated protection equipment and tools;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground must, before it is attached to
    isolated electrical equipment, be attached to a point of safety
    grounding; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground must, before it is disconnected
    from the point of safety grounding, be removed from the isolated
    electrical equipment in a manner that the employee avoids contact
    with all live conductors.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (6), a [[point of
  safety grounding]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a grounding bus, a
  station grounding network, a neutral conductor, a metal structure or
  an aerial ground.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 302](#1178254-1189916){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178254-1189916.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 16]{.HLabel1}[Hot Work Operations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756845 .Part}

### [Definition]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756846 .Subheading}

**[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, [[hot
work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any work where flame is used or a
source of ignition may be produced.

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756848 .Subheading}

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** If hot work is to be
performed,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a qualified person must be assigned to patrol the
  working area and the adjoining areas and maintain a fire protection
  watch of the area for the duration of the work and, if necessary, as
  determined by the qualified person, for a period of 30 minutes after
  the work is completed; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a sufficient number of fire extinguishers shall be
  provided in the working area and the adjoining areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 51](#1299387-1300480){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299387-1300480.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-190-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 190"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** Hot work must not be
performed in a working area where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} flammable gas, vapour or dust may be present in the
  atmosphere, unless the area has been freed of gas, tested by a marine
  chemist or other qualified person, and found to be safe for that work
  to be performed in the area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an explosive or flammable substance may be present in
  the working area, unless a marine chemist or other qualified person
  has ensured that adequate protection exists to permit that work to be
  safely performed in the area.

<!-- -->

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Electrical welding equipment cables
  and gas welding or burning equipment cylinders and pipes must be
  placed clear of areas used for vehicles unless adequate protection for
  the cables, cylinders and pipes is provided.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Gas cylinders of welding and burning equipment must
  be placed securely in an upright position when in use.

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** Before equipment used
for hot work is left unattended, the person in charge of the working
area must ensure that the equipment is in a safe condition.

### [Ventilation Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756859 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 303(F)

\]
:::

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** If a hazardous
substance may be produced in a work area as a result of hot work

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the work area must be well ventilated; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person in the work area must wear respiratory
  protective equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 304](#1178261-1189926){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178261-1189926.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 15](#1343868-1344433){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343868-1344433.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-194-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 194"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  concentration of any chemical agent to which a person is likely to be
  exposed in a work area must not be more than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the value referred to in subsection 255(1); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the percentage referred to in subsection 255(5).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The concentration in a work area of airborne
  hazardous substances, other than chemical agents, must not be
  hazardous to the health or safety of the person in the area.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 305](#1178263-1189928){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178263-1189928.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-195-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 195"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** The percentage of
oxygen in the atmosphere in a working area must not be less than 19.5
per cent or more than 23 per cent by volume at normal atmospheric
pressure and in no case is the partial pressure of oxygen to be less
than 148 mm Hg.

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  ventilation equipment is used to maintain the concentration of an
  airborne hazardous substance below or at the value or percentage
  referred to in section 195 or the percentage of oxygen in the air of a
  confined space within the limits referred to in section 196, the
  employer is prohibited from granting any person access to the work
  area, unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ventilation equipment is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} equipped with an alarm that will, if the
      equipment fails, be activated automatically and be audible or
      visible to any person in the working area, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} monitored by an employee who is in constant
      attendance at the equipment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the event of a failure of the ventilation
    equipment, sufficient time will be available for a person to escape
    from the working area before one of the following occurs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that person's exposure to or the concentration of
      a hazardous substance in the work area is more than the value or
      percentage prescribed in section 195, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the percentage of oxygen in the atmosphere
      ceases to meet the requirements of section 196.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employee referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(ii)
  must activate an alarm in the event of faulty operation of the
  ventilation equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 306](#1178268-1189932){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178268-1189932.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-197-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 197"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 17]{.HLabel1}[Boilers and Pressure Vessels]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756878 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756879 .Subheading}

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply in respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a heating boiler that has a wetted heating surface of
  3 m^2^ or less;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has a capacity of 40 l or
  less;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that is installed for use at a
  pressure of one atmosphere of pressure or less;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  152 mm or less;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  610 mm or less and that is used to store hot water;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  610 mm or less connected to a water pumping system and that contains
  compressed air which serves as a cushion;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a hydropneumatic tank that has an internal diameter
  of 610 mm or less; or

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} a refrigeration plant that has a capacity of 18 kW or
  less.

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 422\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-199-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 199"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Use, Operation and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756891 .Subheading}

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** An employer must ensure
that a qualified person, charged with the operation of a boiler, is in
attendance and readily available at all times while the boiler is in
operation.

## [PART 18]{.HLabel1}[Tools and Machinery]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756893 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756894 .Subheading}

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[explosive-actuated fastening tool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[explosive-actuated fastening tool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    tool that, by means of an explosive force, propels or discharges a
    fastener for the purpose of impinging it on or causing it to
    penetrate another object or material. ([pistolet de scellement à
    cartouches explosives]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fire hazard area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fire hazard area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area that contains
    or is likely to contain an explosive or flammable concentration of a
    hazardous substance. ([endroit présentant un risque
    d'incendie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 308](#1178281-1189939){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178281-1189939.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-201-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 201"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756898 .Subheading}

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to
machines, tools and guards.

### [Design, Construction and Operation of Tools]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756900 .Subheading}

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  exterior surface of any tool used by an employee in a fire hazard area
  must be made of non-sparking material.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All portable electric tools used by employees must
  meet the CSA Standard CAN/CSA-C22.2 NO. 71.1-M89 (R2004), Portable
  Electric Tools.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} All portable electric tools used by employees must be
  grounded except when the tools

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are powered by a self-contained battery;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a protective system of double insulation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are used in a location where reliable grounding
    cannot be obtained if the tools are supplied from a double insulated
    portable ground fault circuit interrupter of the class A type that
    meets the standards set out in CSA Standard CAN/CSA-C22.2 NO.
    144-M91 (R2006), Ground Fault Circuit Interrupters.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} All portable electric tools used by employees in a
  fire hazard area must be marked as appropriate for use or designed for
  use in a fire hazard area.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an air hose is connected to a portable air-powered
  tool used by an employee, a restraining device must be attached

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to all hose connections; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an employee may be injured by the tool falling,
    to the tool.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} All explosive-actuated fastening tools used by
  employees must meet and be operated in accordance with the standards
  set out in CSA Standard Z166-1975, Explosive Actuated Fastening Tools.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} An employee must not operate an explosive-actuated
  fastening tool unless authorized to do so by their employer.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} All chain saws used by employees must meet the
  standards set out in CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z62.1-03 (R2008), Chain
  Saws.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 309(F)](#1178286-1189942){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178286-1189942.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 203"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Defective Tools and Machinery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756915 .Subheading}

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an employee finds any defect in a tool or machine that may render it
  unsafe for use, they must report the defect to their employer as soon
  as feasible.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must mark or tag as unsafe and remove
  from service any tool or machine used by employees that has a defect
  that may render it unsafe for use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 310](#1178291-1189944){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178291-1189944.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-204-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 204"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Training and Instruction]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756919 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 311(F)

\]
:::

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee must be trained and
  instructed by a qualified person in the safe and proper inspection,
  maintenance and use of all tools and machines that they are required
  to use.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must keep a manual of operating
  instructions for each type of portable electric tool, portable
  air-powered tool, explosive-actuated fastening tool and machine used
  by employees and that manual must be made readily available for
  examination by employees who are required to use the tool or machine
  to which the manual applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 312(F)](#1178298-1189950){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178298-1189950.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 205"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General Requirements for Protective Guards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756923 .Subheading}

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every machine that has exposed
  moving, rotating, electrically charged or hot parts or that processes,
  transports or handles material that constitutes a hazard to an
  employee must be equipped with a machine guard that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents the employee or any part of their body
    from coming into contact with the parts or material;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevents access by the employee to the area of
    exposure or to the material or parts that constitute a hazard during
    the operation of the machine; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} makes the machine inoperative if the employee or
    any part of their clothing is in or near a part of the machine that
    is likely to cause injury.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If feasible, the machine guard referred to in
  subsection (1) must not be removable.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A machine guard must be constructed, installed and
  maintained so that it meets the requirements of subsection (1).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Equipment used in the mechanical transmission of
  power must meet the requirements set out in ANSI Standard ANSI/AMT
  B15.1-2000 (R2008), Safety Standard for Mechanical Power Transmission
  Apparatus.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 313](#1178300-1189952){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178300-1189952.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Use, Operation, Repair and Maintenance of Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756932 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2021-122, s. 52(F)

\]
:::

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Machines must be operated, repaired
  and maintained by a qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a machine guard is installed on a machine, it is
  prohibited for any person to use or operate the machine unless the
  machine guard is in its proper position, except to permit the removal
  of an injured person from the machine.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If it is necessary to remove a machine guard from a
  machine in order to perform repair or maintenance work on the machine,
  it is prohibited for any person to perform that work unless the
  machine has been rendered inoperative.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to render the machine
  inoperative, the repair or maintenance work may be performed if the
  employer has established procedures and methods in accordance with
  sections 123 and 124.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 314](#1178305-1189955){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178305-1189955.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Abrasive Wheels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756938 .Subheading}

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Abrasive wheels must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} used only on machines equipped with machine guards;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} mounted between flanges; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} operated in accordance with sections 4 to 6 of CSA
    Standard B173.5--1979, Safety Requirements for the Use, Care and
    Protection of Abrasive Wheels.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A bench grinder must be equipped with a work rest or
  other device that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents the work piece from jamming between the
    abrasive wheel and the wheel guard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not make contact with the abrasive wheel at
    any time.

## [PART 19]{.HLabel1}[Materials Handling and Storage]{.HTitleText1} {#h-756947 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756948 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756949 .Subheading}

**[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[materials handling equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[materials handling equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means equipment
    used to transport, lift, move or position materials, goods or things
    and includes mobile equipment, but does not include a persons
    transfer apparatus within the meaning of section 129. ([appareil de
    manutention des matériaux]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[National Fire Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[National Fire Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National Fire
    Code of Canada 2005, issued by the Associate Committee on the
    National Fire Code, National Research Council of Canada. ([Code
    national de prévention des incendies]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who controls the
    operation of motorized or manual materials handling equipment and
    who has received or is receiving instructions and training in
    respect of the procedures referred to in subsections 228(1) or (3),
    as the case may be. ([opérateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[safe working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[safe working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to
    materials handling equipment, the maximum load that the materials
    handling equipment is designed and constructed to handle or support
    safely. ([charge de travail admissible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[signaller]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[signaller]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person assigned by an
    employer to direct, by means of visual or auditory signals, the safe
    movement and operation of materials handling
    equipment. ([signaleur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 315](#1178307-1189958){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178307-1189958.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756956 .Subheading}

**[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to or in respect of the inspection and certification of tackle
used in the loading or unloading of vessels.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Design and Construction]{.HTitleText2} {#h-756958 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756959 .Subheading}

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment must,
  if feasible, be designed and constructed so that, if there is a
  failure of any part of the equipment, it will not result in loss of
  control of the equipment or create a hazardous condition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All glass in doors, windows and other parts of
  materials handling equipment must be of a type designed not to shatter
  into sharp or dangerous pieces on impact.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 316(E)](#1178309-1189961){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178309-1189961.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protection from Falling Objects]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756963 .Subheading}

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  materials handling equipment is used in circumstances where there is a
  risk that the operator of the equipment could be struck by a falling
  object or shifting load, the employer must equip it with a protective
  structure of a design, construction and strength that will, under all
  foreseeable conditions, prevent the penetration of the object or load
  into the area occupied by the operator.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The protective structure must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} constructed from non-combustible or fire-resistant
    material; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed to permit quick exit from the materials
    handling equipment in an emergency.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, during the loading or unloading of materials
  handling equipment, the load will pass over the operator's position,
  the operator must not occupy the equipment unless it is equipped with
  a protective structure referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 317](#1178311-1189964){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178311-1189964.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protection from Overturning]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756970 .Subheading}

- **[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  materials handling equipment is used in circumstances in which it may
  turn over, it must be fitted with a rollover protection device that
  meets the requirements set out in CSA Standard B352.0-09, Roll-over
  protective structures (ROPS), falling object protective structures
  (FOPS), operator protective structures (OPS), and tip-over protective
  structures (TOPS) for mobile machinery - General Canadian
  requirements.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Guards must be installed on the deck of every vessel
  and on every other elevated work area on which materials handling
  equipment is used to prevent the equipment from falling over the sides
  of the deck or area.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A load must not be left suspended from any lifting
  machinery unless a qualified person is present and in charge of the
  machinery while the load is left suspended.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 318](#1178313-1189967){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178313-1189967.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-213-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 213"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fuel Tanks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756975 .Subheading}

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** If a fuel tank,
compressed gas cylinder or similar container contains a hazardous
substance and is mounted on materials handling equipment, it must be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} located or protected with guards so that under all
  conditions it is not hazardous to the health or safety of an employee
  who is required to operate or ride on the materials handling
  equipment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} connected to fuel overflow and vent pipes that are
  located so that fuel spills and vapours cannot

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be ignited by hot exhaust pipes or other hot or
    sparking parts, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} be hazardous to the health or safety of any
    employee who is required to operate or ride on the materials
    handling equipment.

#### [Operator's Compartment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756981 .Subheading}

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized materials handling
  equipment that is regularly used outdoors must be fitted with a roof
  or other structure that will protect the operator from exposure to any
  weather condition that is likely to be hazardous to the operator's
  health or safety.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If heat produced by materials handling equipment may
  raise the temperature in the operator's compartment or position to
  27°C or more, the compartment or position must be protected from the
  heat by an insulated barrier.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 319(F)](#1178318-1189970){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178318-1189970.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 215"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Controls]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756985 .Subheading}

**[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** The arrangement and
design of dial displays and the controls and general layout and design
of the operator's compartment or position on all materials handling
equipment must not hinder or prevent the operator from operating the
materials handling equipment.

#### [Fire Extinguishers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756987 .Subheading}

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment that is
  used for transporting or handling combustible or flammable substances
  must be equipped with a dry chemical fire extinguisher.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The fire extinguisher must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have not less than an A B C rating as defined in
    the National Fire Code;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meet the standards set out in section 6.2 of that
    Code; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be located so that it is easy to reach by the
    operator of the materials handling equipment while they are in the
    operating position.

#### [Means of Entering and Exiting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756994 .Subheading}

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** All materials handling
equipment must be provided with a step, handhold or other means of
entering into and exiting from the compartment or position of the
operator and any other place on the equipment that an employee enters in
order to service the equipment.

#### [Lighting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-756996 .Subheading}

**[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** Materials handling
equipment that is used or operated by an operator in a work place at
night or at any time when the level of lighting within the work place is
less than 10 lx, must be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} fitted with warning lights on its front and rear that
  are visible from a distance of not less than 100 m; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided with lighting that ensures the safe
  operation of the equipment under all conditions of use.

#### [Control Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757000 .Subheading}

**[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** All materials handling
equipment must be fitted with braking, steering and other control
systems that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} are capable of safely controlling and stopping the
  movement of the materials handling equipment and any hoist, bucket or
  other part of the materials handling equipment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respond reliably and quickly to moderate effort by
  the operator.

#### [Warnings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757004 .Subheading}

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized materials handling
  equipment that is used in an area occupied by employees must be fitted
  with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a horn or other similar audible warning device for
    travelling forward at speeds in excess of 8 km/h; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), a horn or other similar
    audible warning device that automatically operates while it travels
    in reverse.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an audible warning device referred to in paragraph
  (1)(b) cannot be clearly heard above the noise of the motorized
  materials handling equipment and any surrounding noise, does not allow
  enough time for a person to avoid the danger in question or does not
  otherwise provide adequate warning, other visual, audible or tactile
  warning devices or methods must be used so that adequate warning is
  provided.

#### [Seat Belts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757010 .Subheading}

**[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** If materials handling
equipment is used under conditions where a seat belt or shoulder strap
type restraining device is likely to contribute to the safety of the
operator or passengers, the materials handling equipment is to be fitted
with belts or devices.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 320(F)](#1178320-1189972){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178320-1189972.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Rear View Mirror]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757012 .Subheading}

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** If materials handling
equipment cannot be operated safely in reverse unless it is equipped
with an outside rear view mirror, the materials handling equipment must
be so equipped.

#### [Electric Materials Handling Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757014 .Subheading}

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** Any materials handling
equipment that is electrically powered must be designed and constructed
so that the operator and all other employees are protected from
electrical shock or injury by means of protective guards, screens or
panels secured by bolts, screws or other equally reliable fasteners.

#### [Automatic Materials Handling Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757016 .Subheading}

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** If materials handling
equipment that is controlled or operated by a remote or automatic system
may make physical contact with an employee, it must be prevented from
doing so by the provision of an emergency stop system or barricades.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 321(F)](#1178322-1189974){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178322-1189974.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-225-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 225"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Conveyors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757018 .Subheading}

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  design, construction, installation, operation and maintenance of each
  conveyor, cableway or other similar materials handling equipment must
  meet the standards set out in ANSI Standard ANSI/ASME B20.1-2009,
  Safety Standards for Conveyors and Related Equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before a conveyer is put in operation, the employer
  must ensure that guards or other devices are installed in areas where
  there is a risk to the health and safety of a person.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Maintenance, Operation and Use]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757022 .Subheading}

#### [Inspection, Testing and Maintenance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757023 .Subheading}

- **[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before materials handling equipment
  is operated for the first time in a work place, the employer must set
  out in writing instructions for the inspection, testing and
  maintenance of that materials handling equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The instructions must specify the nature and
  frequency of inspections, tests and maintenance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The inspection, testing and maintenance of all
  materials handling equipment must be performed by a qualified person.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The qualified person must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} comply with the instructions referred to in
    subsection (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make and sign a report of each inspection, test or
    maintenance work performed by them.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The report referred to in paragraph (4)(b) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include the date of the inspection, test or
    maintenance performed by the qualified person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the materials handling equipment that was
    inspected, tested or maintained; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the safety observations of the qualified
    person inspecting, testing or maintaining the materials handling
    equipment.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep, on the vessel on which the
  materials handling equipment is located, a copy of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the instructions referred to in subsection (1) for
    as long as the materials handling equipment is in use; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the report referred to in paragraph (4)(b) for a
    period of two years after the day on which the report is signed.

#### [Operator Training and Instruction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757038 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 322(F)

\]
:::

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer must ensure that every operator of motorized materials
  handling equipment has been instructed and trained in the procedures
  to be followed for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inspection of the equipment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the safe and proper use of the equipment in
    accordance with the instructions provided by the manufacturer and
    taking into account the conditions of the work place in which the
    operator will operate the equipment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the fuelling of the equipment, if applicable.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an employer with
  respect to an operator who, under the direct supervision of a
  qualified person, is being instructed and trained in the use of
  motorized materials handling equipment or in the procedures referred
  to in that subsection.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer must ensure that every operator of manual
  materials handling equipment receives on-the-job training by a
  qualified person on the procedures to be followed for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inspection of the equipment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the safe and proper use of the equipment, in
    accordance with any manufacturer's instructions and taking into
    account the condition of the work place in which the operator will
    operate that equipment and the operator's physical capabilities.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must keep a written or electronic
  record of any training or instruction referred to in subsection (1)
  given to an operator of materials handling equipment for as long as
  the operator remains in their employ.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 323](#1178326-1189979){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178326-1189979.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 53](#1299391-1300487){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299391-1300487.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-228-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 228"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Operation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757049 .Subheading}

- **[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer must not require an employee to operate materials handling
  equipment unless the employee is an operator who is capable of
  operating the equipment safely.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not operate materials handling
  equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have a clear and unobstructed view of the area
    in which the equipment is being operated; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are directed by a signaller.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment must not be operated on
  a gangway with a slope greater than the maximum slope recommended by
  the manufacturer of the equipment.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person must not leave materials handling equipment
  unattended unless the equipment has been secured to prevent
  inadvertent movement or unauthorized use.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must establish a code of signals for
  the purposes of paragraph (2)(b) and must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} instruct every signaller and operator of materials
    handling equipment employed by them in the use of the code; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep a copy of the code in a place where it is
    readily available for examination by the signallers and operators.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A signaller must not perform duties other than
  signalling while any materials handling equipment under their
  direction is in motion.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible for a signaller to use visual
  signals, a telephone, radio or other audible signalling device must be
  provided by the employer for the signaller's use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 324](#1178335-1189982){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178335-1189982.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 54](#1299398-1300489){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299398-1300489.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-229-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 229"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Repairs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757062 .Subheading}

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), any
  repair, modification or replacement of a part of any materials
  handling equipment must not decrease the safety of the equipment or
  part.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a part of lesser strength or quality than the
  original part is used in the repair, modification or replacement of a
  part of any materials handling equipment, the use of the equipment
  must be restricted by the employer to such loading and use as will
  ensure the retention of the original safety factor of the equipment or
  part.

#### [Loading, Unloading and Maintenance While in Motion]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757066 .Subheading}

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  load must not be picked up from or placed on any materials handling
  equipment while the equipment is in motion unless the equipment is
  specifically designed for that purpose.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except in the case of an emergency, an employee must
  not get on or off of any materials handling equipment while it is in
  motion.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), repair, maintenance or
  cleaning work must not be performed on any materials handling
  equipment while the materials handling equipment is being operated.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Fixed parts of materials handling equipment may be
  repaired, maintained or cleaned while the equipment is being operated
  if they are isolated or protected so that the operation of the
  equipment does not affect the safety of the employee performing the
  repair, maintenance or cleaning work.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 325(F)](#1178342-1189985){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178342-1189985.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-231-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 231"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Positioning the Load]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757072 .Subheading}

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  materials handling equipment is travelling with a raised or suspended
  load on a vessel, the operator must ensure that the load is carried as
  close as possible to the deck and that the load must not be carried at
  a point above the centre of gravity of the loaded materials handling
  equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If tools, tool boxes or spare parts are carried on
  materials handling equipment, they must be securely stored.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 326(E)](#1178344-1189987){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178344-1189987.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-232-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 232"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Housekeeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757076 .Subheading}

**[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** The floor, cab and
other occupied parts of materials handling equipment must be kept free
of any grease, oil, materials, tools or equipment that may create a
hazard to an employee.

#### [Parking]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757078 .Subheading}

**[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** Materials handling
equipment must not be parked in a passageway, doorway or other place
where it may interfere with the safe movement of persons, materials,
goods or things.

#### [Materials Handling Area]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757080 .Subheading}

- **[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[materials handling area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
  area, including the area covered by wide swinging booms or other
  similar parts, within which materials handling equipment may create a
  hazard to any person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The main approaches to any materials handling area
  must be posted with warning signs or must be under the control of a
  signaller while operations are in progress.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person must not enter a materials handling area
  while operations are in progress unless that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is the Head of Compliance and Enforcement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is an employee whose presence in the materials
    handling area is essential to the conduct, supervision or safety of
    the operations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a person who has been assigned by the employer
    to be in the materials handling area while operations are in
    progress.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If any person other than a person referred to in
  subsection (3) enters a materials handling area while operations are
  in progress, the employer must ensure that the operations in that area
  are immediately discontinued and not resumed until that person has
  left the area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-148, s. 31
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 327](#1178346-1189989){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178346-1189989.html"}
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 11](#1295174-1312267){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295174-1312267.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-235-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 235"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Dumping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757091 .Subheading}

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  materials handling equipment designed for dumping is used to discharge
  a load at the edge of a sudden drop in level that may cause the
  equipment to tip, a bumping block must be used or a signaller must
  give directions to the operator of the equipment to prevent it from
  falling over the edge.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer who wishes to use signals to direct
  the movement of motorized materials handling equipment must establish
  a single code of signals to be used by signallers in all of the
  employer's work places.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A signal to stop given by any person granted access
  to the work place by the employer must be obeyed by the operator.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A signaller must not perform duties other than
  signalling while the motorized materials handling equipment under the
  signaller's direction is in operation.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If any movement of motorized materials handling
  equipment that is directed by a signaller poses a risk to the safety
  of any person, the signaller must not give the signal to move until
  that person is warned of, or protected from, the risk.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of any motorized materials handling
  equipment does not understand a signal, the operator must consider
  that signal to be a stop signal.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2019-246, s. 328\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 328](#1178351-1189992){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178351-1189992.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 55](#1299400-1300491){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299400-1300491.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-236-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 236"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Gradients]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757100 .Subheading}

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** An employee must not
operate and the employer must not permit an employee to operate
motorized materials handling equipment on a gangway with a gradient in
excess of the lesser of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the gradient that is recommended as safe by the
  manufacturer of the motorized materials handling equipment, whether it
  is loaded or unloaded, as the case may be, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gradient that a qualified person ascertains is
  safe, having regard to the mechanical condition of the motorized
  materials handling equipment and its load and traction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 329(F)](#1178352-1189995){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178352-1189995.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-237-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 237"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Enclosed Working Areas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757104 .Subheading}

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every enclosed working area in which
  materials handling equipment powered by an internal combustion engine
  is used must be ventilated in a manner that the carbon monoxide
  concentration in the atmosphere of the working area is not more than
  the threshold limit values as set out by the most recent edition of
  the American Conference of Governmental Industrial Hygienists
  publication entitled Threshold Limit Values (TLVs) and Biological
  Exposure Indices (BEIs).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep a record of the date, time,
  location and results of carbon monoxide tests for every enclosed
  working area in which materials handling equipment powered by an
  internal combustion engine is used.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The records must be available for inspection for a
  period of at least 30 days after the day on which the record was
  created.

#### [Fuelling]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757109 .Subheading}

- **[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  materials handling equipment is fuelled on a vessel, the fuelling is
  to be done in accordance with the instructions given by the employer
  under paragraph 228(1)(c) in a place where the vapours from the fuel
  are readily dissipated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an employee must not fuel
  materials handling equipment

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the hold or an enclosed space of a vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the engine of the equipment is running; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there is any source of ignition in the vicinity
    of the equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment may be fuelled in the
  hold or an enclosed space of a vessel if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one employee is in the hold or space with a
    suitable fire extinguisher ready for use;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} only those employees engaged in the fuelling and
    the employee referred to in paragraph (a) are in the hold or space;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} only the minimum quantity of fuel needed to fill
    the fuel tank of the materials handling equipment is taken into the
    hold or space at one time;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the fuel is liquified gas, the materials
    handling equipment is fuelled only by the replacement of spent
    cylinders; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} fuel is not transferred into containers other than
    the fuel tank of the materials handling equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 330(F)](#1178354-1189997){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178354-1189997.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 56](#1299402-1300493){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299402-1300493.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-239-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 239"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Safe Working Loads]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757122 .Subheading}

- **[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment must
  not be used or operated with a load that is in excess of its safe
  working load.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), the safe working
  load of materials handling equipment must be clearly marked on the
  equipment or on a label securely attached to a permanent part of the
  equipment in a position where the mark or label can be easily read by
  the operator of the equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If derricks are certified for and marked with a safe
  working load for operation in union purchase, the load lifted when in
  union purchase must not be more than that safe working load.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If derricks are operated in union purchase and they
  are not certified and marked in accordance with subsection (3)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the load lifted must not be in excess of one-half
    of the safe working load of the derrick with the smaller capacity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the angle formed by the cargo runners must not be
    more than 120°; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the attachments and fittings of the cargo runners,
    guy wires and preventers must be suitable for the loads to which
    they are subjected.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 57(F)](#1299407-1300495){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299407-1300495.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 240"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Clearances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757131 .Subheading}

- **[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  any route that is regularly travelled by materials handling equipment,
  the overhead and side clearances must be adequate to permit the
  equipment and its load to be manoeuvred safely by an operator.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment must not be operated in
  an area in which it may contact an electrical cable, pipeline or other
  overhead hazard known to the employer, unless the operator has been

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} warned of the presence of the hazard;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} informed of the location of the hazard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} informed of the safety clearance that must be
    maintained with respect to the hazard in order to avoid accidental
    contact with it.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Manual Handling of Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757138 .Subheading}

- **[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  because of the weight, size, shape, toxicity or other characteristic
  of materials, goods or things, the manual handling of them may be
  hazardous to the health or safety of an employee, the employer must
  issue instructions that the materials, goods or things are, if
  feasible, not to be handled manually.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an employee is required to manually lift or carry
  a load in excess of 10 kg, the employer must train and instruct the
  employee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a safe method of lifting and carrying the load;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a work procedure appropriate to the employee's
    physical condition and the conditions of the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 331](#1178356-1189999){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178356-1189999.html"}
:::

## [PART 20]{.HLabel1}[Hazardous Substances]{.HTitleText1} {#h-757144 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757145 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757146 .Subheading}

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[airborne asbestos fibres]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[airborne asbestos fibres]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means asbestos
    fibres that are longer than 5 μm (micrometres) with an aspect ratio
    equal to or greater than 3:1 and that are carried by the
    air. ([fibres d'amiante aéroportées]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[airborne chrysotile asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[airborne chrysotile asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2017-132, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

[[asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means actinolite, amosite,
    anthophyllite, chrysotile, crocidolite and tremolite in their
    fibrous form. ([amiante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[asbestos-containing material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[asbestos-containing material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any article that is manufactured and contains 1%
      or more asbestos by weight at the time of manufacture or that
      contains a concentration of 1% or more asbestos as determined in
      accordance with Method 9002 set out in the document entitled NIOSH
      Manual of Analytical Methods, published by the National Institute
      for Occupational Safety and Health, as amended from time to time,
      or in accordance with a scientifically proven method used to
      collect and analyze a representative sample of the material; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any material that contains a concentration of 1%
      or more asbestos as determined in accordance with Method 9002 set
      out in the document entitled NIOSH Manual of Analytical Methods,
      published by the National Institute for Occupational Safety and
      Health, as amended from time to time, or in accordance with a
      scientifically proven method used to collect and analyze a
      representative sample of the material. ([matériau contenant de
      l'amiante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[clearance air sampling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[clearance air sampling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the action of
    taking samples to determine if the concentration of airborne
    asbestos fibres inside an enclosure is below the limit referred to
    in section 255 to permit the dismantling of a containment
    system. ([échantillonnage de l'air après
    décontamination]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[containment system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[containment system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an isolation system
    that is designed to effectively contain asbestos fibre within a
    designated work area where asbestos-containing material is handled,
    removed, encapsulated or enclosed. ([système de
    confinement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[encapsulation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[encapsulation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the treatment of an
    asbestos-containing material with a sealant that penetrates the
    material and binds the asbestos fibres together, and the treatment
    of the surface of the asbestos-containing material with a sealant
    that creates a membrane on the surface, to prevent the release of
    asbestos fibres into the air. ([encapsulation]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[enclosure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[enclosure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a physical barrier such as
    drywall, plywood or metal sheeting that, as part of the containment
    system, isolates asbestos-containing material from adjacent areas in
    a building to prevent the release of airborne asbestos fibres into
    those areas. ([encloisonnement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[friable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[friable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of
    asbestos-containing material, that the material, when dry, can be
    easily crumbled or powdered by hand
    pressure. ([friable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[glove bag]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[glove bag]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a polyethylene or polyvinyl
    chloride bag that is affixed around an asbestos-containing source
    and that permits asbestos-containing material to be removed while
    minimizing the release of airborne asbestos fibres into the work
    place. ([sac à gants]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hazard information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hazard information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
    hazardous substance, information on the proper and safe storage,
    handling, use and disposal of the hazardous substance, including
    information relating to the health and physical hazards that it
    presents. ([renseignements sur les risques]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[HEPA filter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[HEPA filter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a high-efficiency
    particulate air filter that has been tested to ensure efficiency
    equal to or exceeding 99.97% for removal of airborne particles
    having a mean aerodynamic diameter of 0.3 µm (micrometres) from the
    air. ([filtre HEPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[high-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[high-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity that
    involves the handling or disturbance of friable asbestos-containing
    material or is carried out in proximity to friable
    asbestos-containing material, that requires a high level of control
    to prevent exposure to excessive concentrations of airborne asbestos
    fibres and that includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the removal or disturbance of more than 1 m^2^ of
      friable asbestos-containing material in a work place, even if the
      activity is divided into smaller jobs,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the spray application of a sealant to a friable
      asbestos-containing material,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the cleaning or removal of air-handling
      equipment, other than filters, in a building that has sprayed-on
      fireproofing or sprayed-on thermal insulation that is
      asbestos-containing material,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the repair, alteration or demolition of all or
      part of a kiln, metallurgical furnace or similar structure that
      contains asbestos-containing material,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the activity is carried out by means of power tools
      that are not attached to dust-collecting devices equipped with
      HEPA filters, and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the repair, alteration or demolition of all or
      part of a building in which asbestos is or was used in the
      manufacture of products, unless the asbestos was cleaned up and
      removed. ([activité à risque élevé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[lower explosive limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[lower explosive limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lower limit
    of flammability of a chemical agent or a combination of chemical
    agents at ambient temperature and pressure, expressed

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a gas or vapour, as a percentage in air by
      volume; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for dust, as the weight of dust per volume of
      air. ([limite explosive inférieure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[low-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[low-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity that
    involves the handling of asbestos-containing material or is carried
    out in proximity to non-friable asbestos-containing material and
    that includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the installation or removal of ceiling tiles that
      are made of non-friable asbestos-containing material and cover an
      area of less than 7.5 m^2^,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the installation or removal of other non-friable
      asbestos-containing material, if the material is not being broken,
      cut, drilled, abraded, ground, sanded or vibrated and dust is not
      being generated,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the material is wetted to control the spread of dust
      or fibres and the activity is carried out only by means of
      non-powered hand-held tools, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the removal of less than 1 m² of drywall in which
      joint cement containing asbestos has been used. ([activité à
      faible risque]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[moderate-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[moderate-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity
    that involves the handling of asbestos-containing material or is
    carried out in proximity to friable asbestos-containing material,
    that is not otherwise classified as a low-risk activity or high-risk
    activity and that includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the removal of all or part of a false ceiling to
      gain access to a work area, if asbestos-containing material is
      likely to be found on the surface of the false ceiling,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the removal or disturbance of 1 m^2^ or less of
      friable asbestos-containing material during repair, alteration,
      maintenance or demolition work in a work place,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enclosure of friable asbestos-containing
      material,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the application of tape, sealant or other
      covering to pipe or boiler insulation that is asbestos-containing
      material,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the removal of ceiling tiles that are
      asbestos-containing material, if the tiles cover an area of
      greater than 2 m^2^ and are removed without being broken, cut,
      drilled, abraded, ground, sanded or vibrated,

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the material is not wetted to control the spread of
      dust or fibres and the activity is carried out only by means of
      non-powered hand-held tools,

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the removal of 1 m^2^ or more of drywall in which
      joint cement that is asbestos-containing material has been used,

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the activity is carried out by means of power tools
      that are attached to dust-collecting devices equipped with HEPA
      filters,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the removal of insulation that is
      asbestos-containing material from a pipe, duct or similar
      structure using a glove bag, and

    - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the cleaning or removal of filters used in
      air-handling equipment in a building that has sprayed-on
      fireproofing that is asbestos-containing material. ([activité à
      risque modéré]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[product identifier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[product identifier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
    in subsection 1(1) of the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17). ([identificateur de
    produit]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2025-79, s. 29\]]{.Repealed}

[[supplier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[supplier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section
    2 of the [Hazardous Products
    Act](/eng/acts/H-3). ([fournisseur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[work activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[work activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any low-risk activity,
    moderate-risk activity or high-risk activity or any activity that is
    ancillary to that activity, and the supervision of that activity and
    that ancillary activity. ([activité de travail]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote

  ajax-after="1520629-1525138.html"}
