#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757194 .Subheading}

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to the transportation or handling of dangerous goods to which the
[Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act, 1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01) and its
regulations apply.

**[[244.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244.1 .sectionLabel}** Every employer must
keep a record of all hazardous substances that are used, produced,
handled or stored for use in the work place, and may either keep such a
record in the work place or keep a centralized record in respect of
several work places in one work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 58](#1299409-1300498){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299409-1300498.html"}
:::

#### [Hazard Investigation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757196 .Subheading}

- **[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the health or safety of an employee is likely to be endangered by
  exposure to a hazardous substance in a work place, the employer shall,
  without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a marine chemist or other qualified person
    to carry out an investigation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify the work place committee or the health and
    safety representative, as the case may be, of the proposed
    investigation and of the name of the person appointed to carry out
    that investigation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the investigation, the following criteria must be
  taken into consideration:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the chemical, biological and physical properties of
    the hazardous substance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the routes of exposure to the hazardous substance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the effects on health of exposure to the hazardous
    substance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the state, concentration and quantity of the
    hazardous substance handled;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the hazardous substance is
    handled;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the control methods used to eliminate or reduce
    exposure to the hazardous substance;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} whether the percentage of oxygen is within the
    range prescribed in section 196;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the value, level or percentage of the hazardous
    substance to which an employee is likely to be exposed; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether the value, level or percentage referred to
    in paragraph (h) is likely to be more than that prescribed in
    sections 195 or 255.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the investigation referred to in
  subsection (1) and after consultation with the work place committee or
  the health and safety representative, as the case may be, the marine
  chemist or other qualified person must set out in a written report
  signed by that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person's observations respecting the criteria
    considered in accordance with subsection (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person's recommendations respecting the manner
    of compliance with sections 246 to 257.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep the report for a period of 30
  years after the day on which the marine chemist or other qualified
  person signed the report.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 332](#1178361-1190002){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178361-1190002.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-245-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 245"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Substitution of Substances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757215 .Subheading}

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** No person is to use a
hazardous substance in a work place if a non-hazardous substance or one
that is less hazardous can be used instead.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 333](#1178370-1190007){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178370-1190007.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-246-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 246"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** If the health of
employees in a work place is likely to be endangered by direct skin
contact with a hazardous substance, the employer must provide a wash
area with wash basins supplied with hot and cold water.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 333](#1178370-1190010){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178370-1190010.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-247-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 247"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Ventilation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757218 .Subheading}

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** Every ventilation
system used to control the concentration of an airborne hazardous
substance must be designed, constructed and installed so that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the airborne hazardous substance is a chemical
  agent, the concentration of the substance is not more than the values
  and limits prescribed in subsection 255(1); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the airborne hazardous substance is not a chemical
  agent, the concentration of the substance is not hazardous to the
  health or safety of employees.

#### [Warnings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757222 .Subheading}

**[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** If feasible, the
employer must provide automated warning and detection systems if the
seriousness of any exposure to a hazardous substance requires the use of
those systems.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 334](#1178372-1190013){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178372-1190013.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 59(E)](#1299411-1300500){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299411-1300500.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 249"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Storage, Handling and Use]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757224 .Subheading}

- **[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every hazardous substance stored,
  handled or used in a work place must be stored, handled or used in a
  manner that the hazard related to that substance is reduced to a
  minimum.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), if a hazardous substance
  is stored, handled or used in a work place, any hazard resulting from
  that storage, handling or use must be confined to as small an area as
  possible.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every container for a hazardous substance that is
  used in a work place must be designed and constructed so that it
  protects the employees from any health or safety hazard that is
  created by the hazardous substance.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The quantity of a hazardous substance used or
  processed in a work place must, if feasible, be limited to the minimum
  quantity required.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, in a work place, a hazardous substance is capable
  of combining with another substance to form an ignitable combination
  and a hazard of ignition of the combination by static electricity
  exists, the employer must adopt and implement the standards set out in
  the 2007 edition of the United States National Fire Protection
  Association publication entitled NFPA 77: Recommended Practice on
  Static Electricity.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 335(E)](#1178374-1190015){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178374-1190015.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 250"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Warning of Hazardous Substances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757231 .Subheading}

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** If a hazardous
substance is stored, handled or used in a work place, signs must be
posted in conspicuous places warning every person granted access to the
work place of the presence of the hazardous substance and of any
precautions to be taken to prevent or reduce any hazard of injury to
health.

#### [Assembly of Pipes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757233 .Subheading}

**[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** Every assembly of
pipes, including pipe fittings, valves, pumps, compressors and other
fixed equipment that is used for transferring a hazardous substance from
one location to another must be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} marked, by labelling, colour-coding, placarding or
  any other mode, to identify the hazardous substance being transferred
  and, if appropriate, the direction of the flow; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fitted with control and safety devices to ensure its
  safe operation, maintenance and repair.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 55
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-252-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 252"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Employee Education and Training]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757239 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2016-141, s. 56(E)

\]
:::

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must, in consultation
  with the policy committee or, if there is no policy committee, the
  work place committee or the health and safety representative, develop
  and implement an employee education and training program with respect
  to hazard prevention and control at the work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employee education and training program must
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee who
    handles or is exposed to or who is likely to handle or be exposed to
    a hazardous substance, with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier of the hazardous
      substance,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all hazard information disclosed by the supplier
      or by the employer on a safety data sheet or label,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all hazard information of which the employer is
      aware or ought to be aware,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the observations referred to in paragraph
      245(3)(a),

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the information disclosed on a safety data sheet
      referred to in subsection 258(2) or 262(1) and the purpose and
      significance of that information, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} in respect of hazardous products on board a
      vessel, the information required to be disclosed on a safety data
      sheet and on a label in accordance with sections 262, 263 and 265
      and the purpose and significance of that information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee who
    operates, maintains or repairs an assembly of pipes referred to in
    section 252, with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} every valve and other control and safety device
      connected to the assembly of pipes,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow for the proper and safe
      use of the assembly of pipes, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the significance of the labelling,
      colour-coding, placarding or other modes of identification that
      are used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee who is
    referred to in paragraph (a) or (b), with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow to implement subsections
      250(1), (2) and (5),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow for the safe storage,
      handling, use and disposal of hazardous substances, including
      procedures to be followed in an emergency involving a hazardous
      substance, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow if an employee is
      exposed to fugitive emissions as defined in section 259; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee on the
    procedures to follow to access electronic or paper versions of
    reports, records of education and training given and safety data
    sheets.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must, in consultation with the policy
  committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work place
  committee or the health and safety representative, review the employee
  education and training program at least once a year and, if necessary,
  revise it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whenever there is a change in conditions in respect
    of the presence of hazardous substances in the work place; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever new hazard information in respect of a
    hazardous substance in the work place becomes available to the
    employer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer must keep a paper or electronic record
  of the education and training program given to each employee.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer must make the record readily available
  for examination by the employee in any form, as determined in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative, and keep it for a period of 10 years after the day on
  which the employee ceases to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} handle or be exposed to or be likely to handle or
    be exposed to the hazardous substance; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} operate, maintain or repair the assembly of pipes.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 57
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 336(F)](#1178379-1190018){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178379-1190018.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-253-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 253"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Medical Examinations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757270 .Subheading}

- **[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the report referred to in subsection 245(3) contains a recommendation
  for a medical examination, the employer may consult a physician
  regarding that recommendation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the employer consults a physician and the
  physician confirms the recommendation for a medical examination, or if
  an employer does not consult a physician, the employer must not permit
  an employee to handle the hazardous substance in the work place until
  a physician, acceptable to the employee, has examined the employee and
  declared the employee fit for work with the hazardous substance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an employer consults a physician, the employer
  must keep a copy of the physician's conclusion with the report.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The cost of a medical examination referred to in
  subsection (2) is to be borne by the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 337](#1178384-1190020){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178384-1190020.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-254-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 254"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Control of Hazards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757276 .Subheading}

- **[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee must not be exposed to a concentration of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an airborne chemical agent, other than airborne
    grain dust, airborne flour dust, and airborne asbestos fibres, in
    excess of the value for that chemical agent adopted by the most
    recent edition of the American Conference of Governmental Industrial
    Hygienists publication entitled Threshold Limit Values (TLVs) and
    Biological Exposure Indices (BEIs);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} airborne grain dust in excess of 4 mg/m^3^;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} airborne flour dust in excess of 3 mg/m^3^; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an airborne hazardous substance, other than a
    chemical agent, that is hazardous to the health and safety of the
    employee.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An employer must ensure that an employee's exposure
  to a concentration of airborne asbestos fibres is as close to zero as
  possible, but in any event the employer must ensure that the
  concentration does not exceed the value for airborne asbestos fibres
  adopted by the American Conference of Governmental Industrial
  Hygienists in its publication entitled Threshold Limit Values (TLV)
  and Biological Exposure Indices (BEI), as amended from time to time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the concentration of an airborne chemical agent is
  likely to exceed the value referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b), if
  the concentration of airborne asbestos fibres is likely to exceed zero
  or if there is a concentration of an airborne hazardous substance that
  is hazardous to the health and safety of the employee, air samples
  must be taken by a qualified person and the concentration of the
  chemical agent, airborne asbestos fibres or hazardous substance must
  be determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the NIOSH standards set out in the 5^th^ edition of
    the NIOSH Manual of Analytical Methods (NMAM);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a method set out in the United States Federal
    Register, volume 40, number 33, dated February 18, 1975, as amended
    by volume 41, number 53, dated March 17, 1976;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a method that collects and analyzes a
    representative sample of the chemical agent with accuracy and with
    detection levels at least equal to those which would be obtained if
    the standards referred to in paragraph (a) were used; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if no specific standards for the chemical agent are
    listed in the standards referred to in paragraph (a) and no method
    is available under paragraph (b) or (c), a scientifically proven
    method used to collect and analyze a representative sample of the
    chemical agent.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A paper or electronic record of each test made under
  subsection (2) must be kept by the employer on board the vessel where
  the concentration was sampled for a period of three years after the
  day on which the test was made.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A record must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the test;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the chemical agent for which the test was made;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the sampling and testing method used;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the result obtained; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name and occupation of the qualified person who
    made the test.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), the concentration of an
  airborne chemical agent or combination of airborne chemical agents in
  a work place must be less than 50 per cent of the lower explosive
  limit of the chemical agent or combination of chemical agents.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a source of ignition may ignite an airborne
  chemical agent or combination of airborne chemical agents in a work
  place, the concentration of the chemical agent or the combination of
  chemical agents must not exceed 10% of the lower explosive limit of
  the chemical agent or combination of chemical agents.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 58
- SOR/2017-132, s. 14
- SOR/2018-137, s. 2
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 338](#1178386-1190022){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178386-1190022.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 30](#1520630-1525225){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520630-1525225.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-255-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 255"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Compressed air, gas or steam must
  not be used for blowing dust or other substances from structures,
  machinery or materials if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is a risk of any person being directly
    exposed to the jet or if a fire, explosion, injury or health hazard
    is likely to result from that use; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that use would result in a concentration of an
    airborne chemical agent that is in excess of the values referred to
    in paragraph 255(1)(a) or subsection 255(1.1) or the limits referred
    to in subsections 255(5) or (6).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Compressed air must not be used for cleaning clothing
  contaminated with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} asbestos; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hazardous substance having an exposure limit
    referred to in paragraph 255(1)(a) or (b) lower than 1 mg/m^3^.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If compressed air is used to clean clothing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} eye protection must be worn as set out in Part 10;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum compressed air pressure in the line is
    to be 69 kPa or a safety nozzle limiting the air pressure to no more
    than 69 kPa must be used.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2021-122, s. 60\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 15
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 339](#1178391-1190025){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178391-1190025.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 60](#1299413-1300503){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299413-1300503.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-256-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 256"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Explosives]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757313 .Subheading}

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** All blasting using
dynamite, blasting caps or other explosives must be done by a qualified
person who, if required under the laws of the province in which the
blasting is carried out, holds a blasting certificate or any other
authorization that may be required under those laws.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Hazardous Substances Other than Hazardous Products]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757315 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2016-141, s. 68

\]
:::

#### [Asbestos Exposure Management Program]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757318 .Subheading}

##### [Asbestos-containing Material]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757319 .Subheading}

- **[[257.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If asbestos-containing material is
  present in a work place and there is the potential for a release of
  asbestos fibres or employee exposure to asbestos fibres, an employer
  must ensure that the qualified person who is carrying out a hazard
  investigation under section 245 takes into consideration the type of
  asbestos, the condition of the asbestos-containing material, the
  friability of the asbestos-containing material, the accessibility to
  and likelihood of damage to the asbestos-containing material and the
  potential for the release of asbestos fibres or employee exposure to
  asbestos fibres.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} At the completion of an investigation carried out
  under section 245, the employer must ensure that a record of the
  location, friability and condition of the asbestos-containing material
  and the type of asbestos contained in that material is kept and made
  readily available for examination by employees and is in any form as
  determined in consultation with the policy committee or, if there is
  no policy committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 340](#1178396-1190027){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178396-1190027.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257.1-20170620.html "Link to previous version of section 257.1"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Asbestos Exposure Control Plan]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757325 .Subheading}

**[[257.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.2 .sectionLabel}** Before undertaking
any work activity that involves asbestos-containing material, an
employer must, in consultation with the policy committee or, if there is
no policy committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
representative, develop, implement and administer an asbestos exposure
control plan that requires the employer to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that a hazard investigation under section 245
  has been carried out by a qualified person and, in the event that
  there is a change in the work activity, review any report that was
  prepared as a result of the investigation and, if necessary, have a
  qualified person carry out another investigation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that a qualified person classifies the work
  activity as a low-risk activity, moderate-risk activity or high-risk
  activity;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all asbestos-containing material present
  in the work place that is exposed or that will be disturbed is
  identified by signs and labels or by any other effective manner;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all friable asbestos-containing material
  present in the work place is controlled by removal, enclosure or
  encapsulation or by any other effective manner to prevent employee
  exposure to asbestos;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensure that procedures and control measures for
  moderate-risk activities and high-risk activities are developed and
  implemented; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} develop and implement an employee education and
  training program that is specific to asbestos-containing material.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

**[[257.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.3 .sectionLabel}** If an employee who
is undertaking automotive service procedures may be exposed to asbestos
from friction material or dust arising from that material, an employer
must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the use of compressed air, brushes or similar means
  to dry-remove friction material dust from automotive assemblies is
  prohibited; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} signs to advise employees of the hazards and required
  precautions are posted in service work areas where friction material
  is handled or dust arising from that material is generated.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

##### [Asbestos Dust, Waste and Debris Removal]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757340 .Subheading}

- **[[257.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} During any work activities that
  involve friable asbestos-containing materials, an employer shall
  ensure that the following activities are carried out frequently and at
  regular intervals as determined by a qualified person, at the end of
  each work shift and immediately after the work activity is completed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all asbestos dust, waste and debris are removed by
    vacuuming with a vacuum cleaner that is equipped with a HEPA filter,
    damp-mopping or wet-sweeping the area that is contaminated with the
    asbestos dust, waste or debris; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any drop sheets that are contaminated with asbestos
    dust, waste or debris are wetted.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All asbestos dust, waste or debris and any drop
  sheets that are contaminated with asbestos dust, waste or debris must
  be placed in a container referred to in section 257.92.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

**[[257.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.5 .sectionLabel}** If a glove bag is
used for the removal of asbestos insulation from pipes, ducts and
similar structures, an employer must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the glove bag is sealed to prevent the release of
  asbestos fibres into the work area;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the glove bag is inspected for damage or defects
  immediately before it is attached to the pipe, duct or similar
  structure and at regular intervals during its use;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} all waste from asbestos-containing material that is
  on surfaces is washed to the bottom of the glove bag and all exposed
  asbestos-containing material is encapsulated when it is inside the
  glove bag;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the glove bag is evacuated using a vacuum cleaner
  that is equipped with a HEPA filter to remove the air inside the bag
  prior to the removal of the glove bag; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} after the glove bag is removed, all exposed surfaces
  are cleaned with a damp cloth and a vacuum cleaner that is equipped
  with a HEPA filter.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

##### [Decontamination]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757356 .Subheading}

- **[[257.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before leaving a work area that is
  contaminated with asbestos-containing material, an employee must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if their protective clothing is to be reused,
    decontaminate the clothing with a damp cloth or a vacuum cleaner
    that is equipped with a HEPA filter before taking the clothing off;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if their protective clothing is not to be reused,
    place the clothing in a container referred to in section 257.92.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must provide employees with a facility
  reserved for washing their hands and face, and employees must wash
  their hands and face using that facility before leaving a work area
  that is contaminated with asbestos-containing material.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

**[[257.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.7 .sectionLabel}** As soon as feasible
after any work activity that involves asbestos-containing material is
completed, an employee must clean reusable tools, equipment, rigid
barriers and portable enclosures that are contaminated with asbestos
with a damp cloth or a vacuum cleaner that is equipped with a HEPA
filter.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 341(E)](#1178398-1190029){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178398-1190029.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257.7-20170620.html "Link to previous version of section 257.7"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Air Sampling]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757367 .Subheading}

- **[[257.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer must ensure that a
  qualified person takes air samples to test for airborne asbestos
  fibres

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the vicinity of the containment system during
    any work activity that involves asbestos-containing material and, in
    the case of a work activity that lasts longer than 24 hours, at
    least daily;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the clean room during removal and clean-up
    operations and, in the case of removal and clean-up operations that
    last longer than 24 hours, at least daily; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in contaminated areas that are inside the
    containment system as necessary during removal and clean-up
    operations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that the following air
  samples are taken:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} two samples for every area in an enclosure that is
    10 m^2^ or less;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} three samples for every area in an enclosure that
    is more than 10 m^2^ and not more than 500 m^2^; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} five samples for every area in an enclosure that is
    more than 500 m^2^.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 24 hours after obtaining the air sampling test
  results, the employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} post a copy of the results in a conspicuous place
    in the work place; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make the results available to the policy committee,
    if any, the work place committee and the health and safety
    representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

##### [Clearance Air Sampling]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757382 .Subheading}

- **[[257.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before dismantling a containment
  system and after all asbestos dust, waste and debris have been cleaned
  up, removed or encapsulated, an employer must ensure that clearance
  air samples are taken inside the enclosure and that the concentration
  of airborne asbestos fibres is determined in accordance with Method
  7400 set out in the document entitled NIOSH Manual of Analytical
  Methods, published by the National Institute for Occupational Safety
  and Health, as amended from time to time, or in accordance with a
  scientifically proven method used to collect and analyze a
  representative sample of airborne asbestos fibres.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} When conducting clearance air sampling, the employer
  shall ensure that forced air is used inside the enclosure to dislodge
  any asbestos fibres from all surfaces and keep them airborne.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Clearance air sampling shall be taken until the
  concentrations of airborne asbestos fibres do not exceed the values
  referred to in subsection 255(1.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

**[[257.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.91 .sectionLabel}** Within 24 hours
after obtaining the clearance air sampling test results, the employer
must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} post a copy of the results in a conspicuous place in
  the work place; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} make the results available to the policy committee,
  if any, the work place committee and the health and safety
  representative, and provide a copy of the results to the Head of
  Compliance and Enforcement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 11](#1295174-1312269){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295174-1312269.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257.91-20170620.html "Link to previous version of section 257.91"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Containers for Asbestos Dust, Waste and Debris]{.HTitleText4} {#h-757394 .Subheading}

**[[257.92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.92 .sectionLabel}** Containers for
the containment of asbestos dust, waste and debris and
asbestos-containing material must be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} dust-tight;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} suitable to contain asbestos dust, waste or debris;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} impervious to asbestos;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} identified as containing asbestos dust, waste or
  debris;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} cleaned with a damp cloth or a vacuum cleaner that is
  equipped with a HEPA filter immediately before being removed from the
  work area; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} removed from the work place frequently and at regular
  intervals as determined by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-132, s. 16
:::

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every container of a hazardous
  substance, other than a hazardous product, that is stored, handled,
  used or disposed of on a vessel must be labelled in a manner that
  discloses clearly

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the substance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazardous properties of the substance; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the product can be safely
    disposed of.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a safety data sheet in respect of a hazardous
  substance, other than a hazardous product, that is stored, handled or
  used on board a vessel may be obtained from the supplier of the
  hazardous substance, the employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain a copy of the safety data sheet; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make the copy of the safety data sheet readily
    available for examination by employees in any form, as determined in
    consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
    committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
    representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, ss. 59, 68
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-258-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 258"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Hazardous Products]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757414 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2016-141, s. 68

\]
:::

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757417 .Subheading}

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[bulk shipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[bulk shipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    subsection 5.5(1) of the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17). ([expédition en
    vrac]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[container]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[container]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any package or receptacle
    including a bag, barrel, bottle, box, can, cylinder, drum and
    storage tank. ([contenant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fugitive emission]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fugitive emission]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a hazardous product
    in gas, liquid, solid, vapour, fume, mist, fog or dust form that
    escapes from processing equipment, from control emission equipment
    or from a product into the work place. ([émission
    fugitive]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hazardous waste]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hazardous waste]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a hazardous product
    that is acquired or generated for recycling or recovery or is
    intended for disposal. ([résidu dangereux]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[laboratory sample]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[laboratory sample]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    subsection 5(1) of the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17). ([échantillon pour
    laboratoire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[significant new data]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[significant new data]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
    in subsection 5.12(1) of the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17). ([nouvelles données
    importantes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[supplier label]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[supplier label]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a label prepared by a
    supplier that discloses any information elements required by the
    [Hazardous Products Act](/eng/acts/H-3). ([étiquette du
    fournisseur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[supplier safety data sheet]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[supplier safety data sheet]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a safety
    data sheet prepared by a supplier that discloses any information
    elements required by the [Hazardous Products
    Act](/eng/acts/H-3). ([fiche de données de sécurité du
    fournisseur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[work place label]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[work place label]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a label prepared by
    an employer in accordance with this Division. ([étiquette du lieu de
    travail]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[work place safety data sheet]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[work place safety data sheet]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a safety
    data sheet prepared by an employer in accordance with subsection
    263(1) or (2). ([fiche de données de sécurité du lieu de
    travail]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 60
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-259-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 259"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757431 .Subheading}

- **[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Division does not apply in respect of any

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} employee employed in the loading or unloading of a
    vessel not registered in Canada, other than an employee employed on
    an uncommissioned vessel of Her Majesty in right of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tobacco or a tobacco product as defined in section
    2 of the [Tobacco and Vaping Products Act](/eng/acts/T-11.5);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} manufactured article as defined in section 2 of the
    [Hazardous Products Act](/eng/acts/H-3); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} wood or a product made of wood.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Division, other than section 272, does not apply
  in respect of hazardous waste.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 61
- 2018, c. 9, s. 77
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-260-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 260"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Safety Data Sheets and Labels in Respect of Certain Hazardous Products]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757441 .Subheading}

**[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 271,
every employer must comply with the same requirements as those set out
in subsection 258(1) in respect of a hazardous product and may, in so
doing, replace the generic name of the product with the brand name,
chemical name, common name or trade name, if the hazardous product

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is present on board a vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} was received from a supplier; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is one of the following:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a hazardous product, other than wood or a product
    made of wood, that is listed in Schedule 1 to the [Hazardous
    Products Act](/eng/acts/H-3),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a nuclear substance, as defined in section 2 of
    the [Nuclear Safety and Control Act](/eng/acts/N-28.3), that is
    radioactive.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 61
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-261-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 261"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Supplier Safety Data Sheets]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757450 .Subheading}

- **[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  hazardous product, other than a hazardous product referred to in
  paragraph 261(c), is received by an employer on board a vessel, the
  employer must, at the time the hazardous product is received, obtain a
  supplier safety data sheet in respect of the hazardous product from
  the supplier, unless the employer is already in possession of a
  supplier safety data sheet that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is for a hazardous product that both has the same
    product identifier and is from the same supplier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} discloses information that is current at the time
    that the hazardous product is received; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} was prepared and dated less than three years before
    the day on which the hazardous product is received.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the supplier safety data sheet in respect of a
  hazardous product on board a vessel is three years old or more, the
  employer must, if feasible, obtain from the supplier a current
  supplier safety data sheet.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible for an employer to obtain a
  current supplier safety data sheet, the employer must update the
  hazard information on the most recent supplier safety data sheet that
  the employer has received, on the basis of the ingredients disclosed
  on that supplier safety data sheet and on the basis of any significant
  new data that the employer is aware.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer is exempt from the requirements of
  subsection (1) if a laboratory sample of a hazardous product is
  received on board a vessel from a supplier who is exempted by the
  [Hazardous Products Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17) from
  the requirement to provide a safety data sheet for that product.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 61
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 342](#1178400-1190031){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178400-1190031.html"}
:::

::: PITLink

Version](section-262-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 262"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Work Place Safety Data Sheets]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757461 .Subheading}

- **[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 271, if an
  employer produces on board a vessel a hazardous product other than a
  fugitive emission or an intermediate product undergoing reaction
  within a reaction or process vessel, the employer must prepare a work
  place safety data sheet in respect of the hazardous product.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 271, if an employer receives a
  supplier safety data sheet, the employer may prepare a work place
  safety data sheet to be used on board a vessel in place of the
  supplier safety data sheet if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the work place safety data sheet discloses at least
    the information disclosed on the supplier safety data sheet;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information disclosed on the work place safety
    data sheet does not disclaim or contradict the information disclosed
    on the supplier safety data sheet;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the supplier safety data sheet is readily available
    for examination by employees on board the vessel in any form, as
    determined in consultation with the policy committee or, if there is
    no policy committee, the work place committee or the health and
    safety representative; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the work place safety data sheet discloses that the
    supplier safety data sheet is available on the vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer must review the accuracy of the
  information disclosed on a work place safety data sheet referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) and update it as soon as feasible after new
  hazard information or significant new data becomes available to the
  employer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the information required to be disclosed on the
  work place safety data sheet is not available or not applicable to the
  hazardous product, the employer must, in place of the information,
  insert the words "not available" or "not applicable", as the case may
  be, in the English version and the words "[non disponible]{.otherLang
  lang="fr"}" or "[sans objet]{.otherLang lang="fr"}", as the case may
  be, in the French version, of the work place safety data sheet.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 61
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 343(E)](#1178405-1190034){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178405-1190034.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-263-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 263"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Availability of Safety Data Sheets]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757473 .Subheading}

- **[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must, in any work
  place in which an employee is likely to handle or be exposed to a
  hazardous product, keep readily available a copy of the work place
  safety data sheet or the supplier safety data sheet, as the case may
  be, in English and in French for examination by employees.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The work place safety data sheet and supplier safety
  data sheet must be made available in any form, as determined in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 61
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 344](#1178407-1190036){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178407-1190036.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-264-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 264"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Labels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757481 .Subheading}

- **[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 266 to 268, each
  hazardous product, other than a hazardous product referred to in
  paragraph 261(c), that is on board a vessel and is intended for use on
  that vessel and each container in which the hazardous product is
  contained on board a vessel must, if the hazardous product or the
  container is received from a supplier, have applied to it a supplier
  label.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 266 to 268 and 271, if a
  hazardous product, other than a hazardous product referred to in
  paragraph 261(c), is received from a supplier and an employer places
  the hazardous product on board a vessel in a container other than the
  container in which it was received from the supplier, the employer
  must apply to the container a supplier label or work place label that
  discloses the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazard information in respect of the hazardous
    product; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that a safety data sheet for
    the hazardous product is on board the vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 266 to 268, if an employer
  produces on board a vessel a hazardous product, other than a fugitive
  emission, and the hazardous product is not in a container, the
  employer must disclose the following information on a work place label
  applied to the hazardous product or on a sign posted in a conspicuous
  place in the work place:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazard information in respect of the hazardous
    product; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that a work place safety
    data sheet for the hazardous product is on board the vessel.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 266 to 268, if an employer
  produces on board a vessel a hazardous product, other than a fugitive
  emission, and places the hazardous product in a container, the
  employer must apply a work place label to the container that discloses
  the information referred to in paragraphs (3)(a) to (c).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 270 and 271, a person must not
  remove, deface, modify or alter the supplier label applied to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a hazardous product that is on board a vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the container of a hazardous product that is on
    board a vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 61
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-265-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 265"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Portable Containers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757498 .Subheading}

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** If an employer stores a
hazardous product on a vessel in a container that has applied to it a
supplier label or a work place label, a portable container filled from
that container does not have to be labelled in accordance with section
265 if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the hazardous product is required for immediate use;
  or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following conditions apply in respect of the
  hazardous product:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it is under the control of and used exclusively by
    the employee who filled the portable container,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} it is used only during the work shift in which the
    portable container was filled, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} it is clearly identified by a work place label
    applied to the portable container that discloses the product
    identifier.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, ss. 62(F), 68
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-266-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 266"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Special Cases]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757507 .Subheading}

**[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** An employer must, in a
conspicuous place near a hazardous product, post a sign in respect of
the hazardous product that discloses the product identifier if the
hazardous product is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a process, reaction or storage vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a continuous-run container;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a bulk shipment that is not placed in a container on
  the vessel; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} not in a container and stored in bulk.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 63
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-267-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 267"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Laboratories]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757515 .Subheading}

- **[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  laboratory sample of a hazardous product is the subject of a labelling
  exemption under subsection 5(5) of the [Hazardous Products
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17), a label that is provided
  by the supplier and is affixed to, printed on or attached to the
  container of the sample received at the work place and that discloses
  the following information in place of the information required under
  paragraph 3(1)(d) of those Regulations is considered to comply with
  the requirements set out in section 265 with respect to a supplier
  label:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if known by the supplier, the chemical name or
    generic chemical name of any material that is in the hazardous
    product and that is classified under the [Hazardous Products
    Act](/eng/acts/H-3) and the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17) as a biohazardous
    infectious material; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the statement "Hazardous Laboratory Sample. For
    hazard information or in an emergency, call/[Échantillon pour
    laboratoire de produit dangereux. Pour obtenir des renseignements
    sur les dangers ou en cas d'urgence, composez]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", followed by an emergency telephone number for the
    purpose of obtaining the information that must be provided on the
    safety data sheet of a hazardous product.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a laboratory sample of a hazardous product is the
  subject of a labelling exemption under subsection 5(6) of the
  [Hazardous Products Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17), a
  label that is provided by the supplier and is affixed to, printed on
  or attached to the container of the sample received at the work place
  and that discloses the following information in place of the
  information required under paragraph 3(1)(c) or (d) of those
  Regulations is considered to comply with the requirements set out in
  section 265 with respect to a supplier label:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if known by the supplier, the chemical name or
    generic chemical name of any material or substance that is in the
    hazardous product and that is referred to in subsection 3(2) of
    Schedule 1 to the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the statement "Hazardous Laboratory Sample. For
    hazard information or in an emergency, call/[Échantillon pour
    laboratoire de produit dangereux. Pour obtenir des renseignements
    sur les dangers ou en cas d'urgence, composez]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", followed by an emergency telephone number for the
    purpose of obtaining the information that must be provided on the
    safety data sheet of a hazardous product.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a hazardous product is in a container other than
  the container in which it was received from a supplier or is produced
  on board a vessel, the employer is exempt from the requirements set
  out in section 265 and subparagraph 266(b)(iii) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer has complied with subsection (4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} employee education and training is provided as
    required by these Regulations; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the hazardous product

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a laboratory sample,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is intended by the employer to be used solely
      for analysis, testing or evaluation in a laboratory, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is clearly identified through any mode of
      identification visible to employees at the work place.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (3)(a), the employer
  shall ensure that the mode of identification used and the employee
  education and training provided enable the employees to readily
  identify and obtain either the information required on a safety data
  sheet or the information set out in subsections (1) and (2) with
  respect to the hazardous product or laboratory sample.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer is exempt from the requirements of
  section 265 if a laboratory sample of a hazardous product is received
  at the work place from a supplier who is exempted by the [Hazardous
  Products Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17) from the
  requirement to provide a label for that product.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 64
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 345](#1178409-1190038){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178409-1190038.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-268-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 268"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757534 .Subheading}

**[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** The information
disclosed on a sign referred to in subsection 265(3), section 267 or
paragraph 272(b) must be of a size that is clearly legible to the
employees in the work place.

#### [Replacing Labels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757536 .Subheading}

- **[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  on board a vessel, a label applied to a hazardous product or the
  container of a hazardous product becomes illegible or is removed from
  the hazardous product or the container, the employer must replace the
  label with a work place label that discloses the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazard information in respect of the hazardous
    product; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that a safety data sheet for
    the hazardous product is available on board the vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must review the accuracy of the
  information on a work place label and update it as soon as feasible
  after new hazard information or significant new data becomes available
  to the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 65
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 346(E)](#1178414-1190041){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178414-1190041.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-270-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 270"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Exemptions from Disclosure]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757545 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2016-141, s. 66(F)

\]
:::

- **[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if an
  employer has filed a claim for exemption from the requirement to
  disclose information on a safety data sheet or on a label under
  subsection 11(2) of the [Hazardous Materials Information Review
  Act](/eng/acts/H-2.7), the employer must disclose, in place of the
  information that the employer is exempt from disclosing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if there is no final disposition of the proceedings
    in relation to the claim, the date on which the claim for exemption
    was filed and the registry number assigned to the claim under
    section 10 of the [Hazardous Materials Information Review
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-456); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the final disposition of the proceedings in
    relation to the claim is that the claim is valid, a statement that
    an exemption has been granted and the date on which the exemption
    was granted.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a claim for exemption is in respect of a product
  identifier, the employer must, on the safety data sheet or label of
  the hazardous product, disclose, in place of that product identifier,
  a code name or code number specified by the employer as the product
  identifier for that hazardous product.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 67
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-271-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 271"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Hazardous Waste]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757555 .Subheading}

- **[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  hazardous product on board a vessel is hazardous waste, the employer
  must disclose the generic name and hazard information in respect of
  the hazardous product by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applying a label to the hazardous waste or its
    container; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} posting a sign in a conspicuous place near the
    hazardous waste or its container.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must provide education and training to
  employees regarding the safe storage and handling of hazardous waste
  that is found in the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-141, s. 67
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 347](#1178416-1190043){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178416-1190043.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-272-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 272"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Information Required in a Medical Emergency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-757563 .Subheading}

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 125.2(1) of the Act, a medical professional is a nurse who is
registered or licensed under the laws of a province.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 348](#1178418-1190047){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178418-1190047.html"}
:::

:::

## [PART 21]{.HLabel1}[Hazardous Occurrence Recording and Reporting]{.HTitleText1} {#h-757565 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757566 .Subheading}

**[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, [[minor
injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employment injury or an
occupational illness for which first aid or medical treatment is
provided, other than a disabling injury.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 33(E)](#1520639-1525283){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520639-1525283.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-274-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 274"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1282744 .Subheading}

**[[274.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274.1 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply in respect of occurrences of harassment and violence in the work
place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-130, s. 51](#1251052-1282747){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1251052-1282747.html"}
:::

### [Employee Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757568 .Subheading}

**[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275 .sectionLabel}** If an employee becomes
aware of an accident or other hazardous occurrence arising in the course
of or in connection with their work that has caused or is likely to
cause injury to that employee or to any other person, the employee must,
without delay, report the accident or occurrence to the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 349(F)](#1178420-1190049){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178420-1190049.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-275-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 275"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Investigation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757570 .Subheading}

**[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** If an employer becomes
aware of an accident, occupational illness or other hazardous occurrence
affecting any of their employees in the course of employment, the
employer must, without delay,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a qualified person to conduct an
  investigation of the hazardous occurrence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify the work place committee or the health and
  safety representative, as the case may be, of the hazardous occurrence
  and of the name of the person appointed to investigate it; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} take necessary measures to prevent a recurrence of
  the hazardous occurrence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 33(E)](#1520639-1525286){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520639-1525286.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-276-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 276"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Immediate Report to Head of Compliance and Enforcement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757575 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-148, s. 32
- SOR/2021-118, s. 11

\]
:::

**[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** The employer must
report to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement the date, time,
location and nature of any accident, occupational illness or other
hazardous occurrence that has one of the following results, as soon as
feasible but not later than 24 hours after becoming aware of that
result:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the death of an employee;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a missing employee;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a disabling injury to two or more employees;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an employee's loss of consciousness as a result of an
  electric shock, a toxic atmosphere or an oxygen deficient atmosphere;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an employee's loss of a body member or a part of one
  or the complete loss of the usefulness of a body member or a part of
  one;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the permanent impairment of an employee's body
  function;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a fire or an explosion;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} damage to a boiler or pressure vessel that results in
  fire or the rupture of the boiler or pressure vessel; or

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} damage to a persons transfer apparatus that renders
  it unserviceable, or a free fall of a persons transfer apparatus.

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2020-130, s. 52\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-148, s. 33
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 350(F)](#1178422-1190050){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178422-1190050.html"}
- [SOR/2020-130, s. 52](#1251054-1282751){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1251054-1282751.html"}
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 11](#1295174-1312275){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295174-1312275.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 16(E)](#1343870-1344435){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343870-1344435.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 33(E)](#1520639-1525288){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520639-1525288.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-277-20220502.html "Link to previous version of section 277"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Minor Injury Record]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757591 .Subheading}

- **[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must keep a record of
  each minor injury of which the employer is aware that is sustained by
  an employee in the course of employment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record must contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the occurrence that
    resulted in the minor injury;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the employee affected;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the minor injury;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the causes of the minor injury; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of the first aid or medical treatment
    given to the employee, if applicable.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 351(E)](#1178427-1190052){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1178427-1190052.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-278-20100603.html "Link to previous version of section 278"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Written Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757600 .Subheading}

- **[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the investigation referred to in section 276 discloses that a
  hazardous occurrence resulted in any of the following circumstances,
  the employer must, without delay after receiving the results of the
  investigation, make a report in writing that meets the requirements of
  subsections (1.1) and (1.2):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the death of an employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a missing employee;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a disabling injury to an employee; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an employee's loss of consciousness as a result of
    electric shock or exposure to a toxic or oxygen-deficient
    atmosphere.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The report must contain the results of the
  investigation and the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the circumstance referred to in subsection (1) that
    was disclosed by the investigation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer's name, mailing address, telephone
    number and [[business number]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
    subsection 248(1) of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and site of the hazardous
    occurrence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the weather conditions at the time of the hazardous
    occurrence, if weather was a contributing factor in the occurrence;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the names of any witnesses to the hazardous
    occurrence;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the employee's supervisor at the time
    of the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the name of the vessel and its official number or
    ID Number;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a description of the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a description of the property damage resulting from
    the hazardous occurrence, if any, and the estimated cost of repair;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} for each injured or ill employee,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} their name, age and gender,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} their occupation and the number of years of
      experience they have in that occupation,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a description of their injury or illness,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the direct cause of their injury or illness, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a description of the training in accident
      prevention that they received in relation to their duties before
      the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the direct causes of the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} a description of each measure taken or to be taken
    by the employer in accordance with paragraph 276(c) and the date of
    its implementation or, if measures are not to be taken, the reasons
    why measures are not necessary;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a description of any other measure taken or to be
    taken by the employer for the purpose of preventing the recurrence
    of the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, telephone number and email address
    of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person appointed to investigate the hazardous
      occurrence, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the work place committee member or health and
      safety representative who participated in the investigation of the
      hazardous occurrence; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} the comments, if any, of the person referred to in
    subparagraph (n)(ii) in relation to the hazardous occurrence, the
    investigation, the corrective measures or the related facts.

- [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The report must be signed and dated by the person
  appointed to investigate the hazardous occurrence and the work place
  committee member or health and safety representative who participated
  in the investigation of the hazardous occurrence.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must submit a copy of the report

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the date of the hazardous
    occurrence, to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement and to the
    Canadian Transportation Accident Investigation and Safety Board; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} without delay, to the work place committee or
    health and safety representative, as the case may be.

### [Annual Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757624 .Subheading}

- **[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer must, not later than
  March 1 in each year, submit to the Minister a written report that
  meets the requirements of subsections (1.1) and (2) regarding the
  accidents, occupational illnesses and other hazardous occurrences of
  which the employer is aware affecting any employee in the course of
  employment during the 12-month period ending on December 31 of the
  preceding year.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The report must be signed and dated by the person
  who prepared it on behalf of the employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report must contain the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the year for which the report is submitted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer's name, mailing address, telephone
    number and [[business number]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
    subsection 248(1) of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each of the employer's work places,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} its address,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the number of employees and the total number of
      hours that they worked,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the number of office employees,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the number, respectively, of minor injuries,
      disabling injuries and deaths, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the number of hazardous occurrences other than
      those referred to in subparagraph (iv); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name, job title, telephone number and email
    address of the person who prepared the report on behalf of the
    employer.

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79, s. 32\]]{.Repealed}

### [Retention of Reports and Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757641 .Subheading}

**[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** Every employer must
keep a copy of each report and record referred to in this Part for a
period of 10 years after the day on which they are made.

## [PART 22]{.HLabel1}[Repeal and Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-757643 .Part}

### [Repeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757644 .Subheading}

**[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282 .sectionLabel}** \[Repeal\]

### [Coming into force]{.HTitleText2} {#h-757646 .Subheading}

**[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations come
into force on the day on which they are registered.

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- SOR/2016-141, s. 77

  - [77]{.lawlabel} If an employer complies with the requirements set
    out in the provisions amended by these Regulations as those
    provisions read immediately before February 11, 2015, the amendments
    made by these Regulations do not apply to the employer

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} during the period that begins on the day on which
      these Regulations come into force and ends on November 30, 2018;
      and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any hazardous products that are in
      the work place on December 1, 2018, during the period that begins
      on December 1, 2018 and ends on May 31, 2019.

- --- SOR/2020-130, s. 53

  - [53]{.lawlabel} Sections 90, 96 and 103 of the [Maritime
    Occupational Health and Safety
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-120), as they read
    immediately before the day on which the [Work Place Harassment and
    Violence Prevention Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-130) come
    into force, continue to apply to all [[work place
    violence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 90 of
    [Maritime Occupational Health and Safety
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-120), and alleged work place
    violence of which the employer becomes aware before the day on which
    [Work Place Harassment and Violence Prevention
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-130) come into force.
